loanDepot (LDI) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Home equity gains offset a volume decline as debt reduction took center stage.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

loanDepot, Inc. (LDI -16.38%) reported a shift in its business model toward higher-margin home equity lending and expanded purchase market share as interest rates remained elevated. Management reported that the company completed significant mortgage servicing rights sales to manage liquidity and reduce total net debt obligations. The company stated that technology automation and process improvements have enhanced operating leverage and unit production efficiency. Executives indicated that the current strategy is designed to drive the organization toward sustainable profitability by the first half of 2027 while scaling its proprietary direct-to-consumer platform.

Operator: Good morning, everyone, and welcome to loanDepot's Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. [Operator Instructions] I would now like to turn the call over to Gerhard Erdelji, Senior Vice President, Investor Relations. Please go ahead.

Gerhard Erdelji: Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining our third quarter 2026 earnings call. Before we begin, I would like to remind everyone that this conference call may include forward-looking statements regarding the company's operating and financial performance in future periods. For more information about factors that may cause actual results to differ materially from forward-looking statements, please refer to the earnings release that we issued earlier today, which is available on our website at investors.loandepot.com. Our presentation today contains certain non-GAAP financial measures that we believe provide additional insight into analyzing and benchmarking the performance and value of our business and facilitating company-to-company operating performance comparisons.

For more details on these non-GAAP financial measures, including reconciliation to the most directly comparable GAAP measures, please refer to today's earnings release. A webcast and a transcript of this call will be posted on our website after the conclusion of this call. On today's call, we have loanDepot's Founder and Chief Executive Officer, Anthony Hsieh; and Chief Financial Officer, David Hayes. They will provide an overview of our quarter, a review of our operating results and our outlook. We are also joined by Chief Investment Officer, Jeff DerGurahian; and Chief Digital Officer, Dominick Marchetti, to help answer your questions after our prepared remarks. And with that, I'll turn things over to Anthony to get us started. Anthony?

Anthony Hsieh: Thank you, Gerhard. I appreciate everyone joining us on the call today. Over the past year, we have remained disciplined in executing our turnaround strategy, which has focused on four priorities: expanding product diversification, increasing market share in attractive origination segments, driving productivity improvements through technology and automation and strengthening our overall financial profile. We believe the progress we made this quarter demonstrates that these initiatives are working despite a significantly more challenging mortgage market that continue to pressure housing activity and industry origination volumes throughout much of 2026. In the third quarter, our team remained focused on executing the strategic initiatives we put in place to strengthen our business across market cycles.

As a result, we delivered meaningful improvements across many of our key operating metrics and continue to gain market share in important areas of the mortgage market. Periods like the current market environment are when competitive advantages begin to reveal themselves. Despite higher rates and lower industry volumes, we grew originations, increased purchase market share, expanded our home equity business and improved operating efficiency compared to a year ago. Since my return last year, we increased origination volume by 8%, grew unit volume by 33%, increased purchase volume by 10%, improved return on marketing by 14% and reduced our cost to produce by 16%.

These results reflect the success of our ongoing efforts to diversify revenue sources, increase operating efficiency and improve the scalability of our platform. One area where we continue to see significant opportunity is home equity lending. Our expansion into home equity lending is diversifying loanDepot beyond traditional rate-driven refinance activity while allowing us to better serve homeowners seeking liquidity without giving up historically low first mortgage rates. Combined with our growing purchase business, these products allow us to serve customers across a broader range of housing and interest rate environments. It also demonstrates the power of our direct-to-consumer channel, which we believe is one of only two scaled branded fintech model direct-to-consumer mortgage platforms in the marketplace.

Our success in home equity reinforces that loanDepot is more than a mortgage originator. We have built a marketing, sales and distribution platform capable of delivering multiple products to customers throughout the homeownership life cycle. As a result of our investments in this business, home equity lending has become an increasingly important contributor to our overall production platform. Based on our analysis of publicly available industry data and internal estimates, we believe loanDepot ranked among the top 3 nonbank home equity lenders during the third quarter based on volume. We accomplished this in less than a year. We believe home equity remains a durable long-term opportunity even if interest rates decline and refinance activity returns.

Millions of homeowners with ultra-low pandemic era mortgage rates are unlikely to benefit from a traditional refinance, and they continue to view home equity products as the preferred way to access liquidity. Importantly, we view home equity as a permanent strategic growth category that complements, rather than replaces, our existing mortgage platform. It expands customer relationships, creates additional revenue opportunities and allows us to engage borrowers regardless of where they are in the interest rate cycle. At the same time, we remain deeply committed to the purchase market. Purchase mortgages continue to represent a critical long-term growth strategy for loanDepot, and we have taken deliberate actions throughout the year to strengthen our position.

We added loan officers and expanded partnerships across both our retail and joint venture channels. These investments contributed to a 9% year-over-year increase in our purchase mortgage market share during the quarter and further strengthened our presence in local communities nationwide. We are also seeing the benefits of our continued investment in technology and process improvement. Our technology investments are delivering measurable operating leverage by automating routine workflows, reducing cycle times, improving customer experience and enabling loanDepot to focus on higher-value activities. These include expanded use of automated underwriting capabilities, digital closing technologies and workflow automation. The combination of greater scale, improved productivity and disciplined expense management continues to enhance operating leverage throughout the organization.

With that, let me turn the call over to Dave to review our financial results in greater detail. Dave?

David Hayes: Thanks, Anthony, and good morning, everyone. The third quarter reflected continued progress against our strategic priorities of improving operating efficiency, expanding revenue opportunities and strengthening our path towards sustainable profitability despite persistent pressure on industry mortgage volumes driven by mortgage rates topping 7%. We reported an adjusted net loss of $31 million in the third quarter compared to an adjusted net loss of $29 million in the second quarter, due primarily to lower adjusted revenue, offset somewhat by lower expenses. Importantly, adjusted net revenue less total expenses improved sequentially, reflecting continued progress in operating performance despite lower volumes.

During the third quarter, our loan origination volume was $7.0 billion for the quarter, a decrease of 12% from the prior quarter's volume of $8.0 billion. This was within the guidance we issued of between $6.25 billion and $8.25 billion. The decline in dollar volume reflects the continued shift toward our 5x5 HomeLoan product, which carries a smaller average loan balance than traditional first lien mortgages. On a unit basis, we increased volume 7% during the quarter, reflecting the success of the product and the investment in our loan officers. Pull-through weighted rate lock volume was $5.6 billion, which represented a 15% decrease from the prior quarter's volume of $6.6 billion.

As a reminder, pull-through weighted rate lock volume is down primarily due to the product mix shift to HELOC, which does not record a lock. Pull-through weighted rate lock volume also came in within the guidance we issued last quarter of $5.25 billion to $7.25 billion and contributed to adjusted total revenue of $302 million, which compared to $307 million in the second quarter. Our pull-through weighted gain on sale margin for the third quarter came in at 399 basis points, exceeding our guidance range of 360 to 390 basis points and up compared to 345 basis points in the prior quarter. Our higher gain on sale margin primarily reflected the product mix shift.

The higher rate environment continued to suppress demand for first trust deed loans, but demand for higher-margin 5x5 HomeLoan HELOC products increased. Their contribution can be seen in the 24% increase in origination income from the prior quarter. Servicing fee income increased from $112 million in the second quarter to $113 million in the third quarter, primarily due to an increase in collections and higher interest credits on escrowed funds. We hedge our servicing portfolio, so we do not record the full impact of the changes in fair value in the results of our operations. We believe this strategy helps protect against volatility in our earnings and liquidity.

Our strategy for hedging the servicing portfolio is dynamic, and we adjust our hedge positions in reaction to the changing interest rate environment. We continue to proactively manage liquidity and our balance sheet. During the quarter, we completed the sale of approximately $10 billion of Agency MSRs. And in October, we sold an additional $9 billion of Government MSRs. Looking forward, we expect for our servicing fee income to decline, reflecting the settlement of these trades. Finally, adjusted revenue was adversely impacted by approximately $10 million due to higher interest rates, resulting in lower fair value marks on our investment in trading securities and increases in our repurchase reserves.

Our total expenses for the third quarter decreased by $8 million from the prior quarter and fell to a greater extent than the decrease in adjusted revenue, reflecting the results of our investment and productivity initiatives. The primary drivers of the decrease from the quarter were lower personnel-related costs and the benefit of debt repurchases during the quarter. Our operating improvements, productivity initiatives and disciplined expense management drove meaningful efficiency gains across the platform. As Anthony mentioned, our cost to produce improved by 16% year-over-year. Meanwhile, our investment in technology is making us more efficient, demonstrated by a unit production per FTE increasing 28% since last year.

As part of our ongoing cost management strategies, we increased the amount of our previously announced productivity initiatives from $12 million to $25 million annualized and taking effect through the remainder of the year. We're continuing to evaluate opportunities for additional productivity improvements. Looking forward to the fourth quarter, we expect pull-through weighted lock volume of between $3.25 billion and $5.25 billion and origination volume of between $4.5 billion and $6.5 billion. These ranges reflect the impact of the seasonal decrease in purchase activity and our belief that the market estimates and the industry volumes are overstated and remain under pressure from the higher interest rate environment.

HELOC volume is not reflected in lock volume, but it is reflected in closed loan volume. It is also worth noting that the shift to HELOC resulted in higher unit volume and contribution margins, albeit at a smaller average loan size. We expect our fourth quarter pull-through weighted gain on sale margin to be between 425 and 465 basis points and our gain on sale margin to be between 315 and 355 basis points. Our total expenses are expected to decrease somewhat in the fourth quarter, primarily due to lower volume-related costs and the impact of our previously announced productivity initiatives. We ended the quarter with $214 million in cash, decreasing by $15 million from the second quarter.

However, we repurchased $87 million of senior notes at an average purchase price of 88% of par during the quarter. Those repurchases, plus the previously announced Agency MSR sale during the quarter, contributed to the reduction in our net debt obligations by $114 million. Finally, we continue to actively work to address our upcoming debt maturities and are pursuing several financing alternatives to refinance or extend our debt with financing counterparties. Our focus is on extending our debt maturity profile, and we'll provide updates as appropriate. We're keenly focused on managing our balance sheet, continuing to focus on maintaining strong liquidity, prudent capital management and operational flexibility.

Looking ahead, we remain committed to driving further efficiency gains while continuing to invest in strategic growth initiatives, including home equity lending, purchase market expansion and technology enhancements. With that, we're ready to turn it back over to the operator for Q&A. Operator?

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Your first question comes from the line of Mihir Bhatia with Bank of America.

Mihir Bhatia: Maybe to start, just on the debt refinancing. Just on the upcoming maturities, is the base case that you extend or refinance those without issuing equity or selling more MSRs? How should we think about the potential increase in interest expense? Any early indications that you can talk about, just from that debt maturity?

David Hayes: Thanks for the question. Yes, unfortunately, I don't think we can elaborate on that too specifically. We -- as I noted in my prepared remarks, we are very actively engaged with our advisers on coming to resolution on that, and we're pursuing several different avenues to make sure we get to the best outcome for the company overall. We're looking at it very holistically. And so it's unclear or we can't share with you at this point how that ultimately resolves, but we hope to have an update in the near term.

Mihir Bhatia: Okay. Understood. Maybe just going to the business and getting to sustainable profitability in this type of interest rate environment. At the midpoint of your fourth quarter volume and margin guidance, how close are you to profitability? And if you're not there, just -- can you talk a little bit about the remaining revenue or expense gap that you need to close? What are you planning for, for next year and to be on a sustainable path if interest rates remain elevated?

Anthony Hsieh: Thanks for the question. This is Anthony. So what we witnessed starting at the end of Q3 was the market backing up further into volume decay, with the 10-year rising above and getting to 5% and above 5%. That, along with seasonality, what we witnessed was an unexpected decline in purchase lending as consumers went into a bit of a sticker shock. Of any particular homebuyers that are out there, some homebuyers actually even canceled their contract as a result of it. So that certainly did not help our momentum. We have been very focused on growing market share since the last year.

And I want to rewind a little bit, just to remind everyone, the organization unfortunately lost quite a bit of market share in recent years. This company was never designed to carry this type of cost structure with the little volumes that we currently enjoyed last year. So we've been heavily on scaling the organization. And our origination channels over the last year, all are pointing very positive and achieving greater market share. Our 3 significant origination channels are builder, which is our joint venture channel. We currently still enjoy being the largest homebuilder financing partner as an independent mortgage bank. We have our retail in-market business that is enjoying positive momentum and growth.

And our fintech consumer direct business has really improved our market share. And we, over the last 6 months, have added the home equity market to the mix, which is completely incremental volume there. So the end of Q3 was both a challenge from a higher interest rate market that presented a surprise. And over the course -- since the conflict started in Iran in March, we've been chasing the market. And even with the market decay, we continue to drive additional revenue and additional market share. So getting over the seasonality of Q4 and Q1, and of course, not knowing where the market will end up next year.

But if it stays at this level, we are very confident that we'll get to a breakeven or profitability by first half or mid next year.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Your next question comes from the line of Mikhail Goberman with Citizens JMP.

Mikhail Goberman: I was wondering if you could speak just broadly on what you're seeing in terms of the competitive landscape out there at the moment? And also, what you're seeing in terms of the credit quality of the borrower, noticing that 60-day plus delinquent rate kind of edging up a little bit? And sort of wondering if there's any wobbles on the horizon that you are seeing and if there are any geographic aspects to that?

Anthony Hsieh: I'll start. Mikhail, great questions. I'll start with answering the credit side, and perhaps if the team has additional comments. We have not or I have not seen any significant changes or deterioration in credit. This is the strongest consumer credit profile that I have witnessed in my career. The overall equity position and credit score is enormously favorable. So we have not seen a change at all, very good thus far. Your question on the competitive side, we're starting to see an adjustment to capacity out there. As volumes continue to shrink and decay, the mortgage industry will adjust.

In this capacity, we're seeing competitors either close down locations, offer itself up for sale or shrink their teams, and we're seeing that in many of our markets today. So that is a healthy process. That just means that margins will most likely act responsibly. And we're seeing some of that stabilize as far as margin, particularly on first mortgages, while second mortgage margins are largely unaffected by interest rate movements.

Operator: There are no further questions at this time. I will now turn the call back to Anthony Hsieh for closing remarks.

Anthony Hsieh: On behalf of Dave, Jeff, Dom and the rest of our team, I want to thank you for joining us today. Since returning as CEO, I have remained focused on accelerating our digital transformation, improving operating efficiency and positioning loanDepot for long-term growth. As we look ahead, we recognize that elevated interest rates continue to present challenges for the broader housing and mortgage market. While the timing of any meaningful improvement in mortgage demand remains uncertain, we are encouraged by the significant progress we have made over the last year. Today, loanDepot is a more diversified, more efficient and more resilient company than it was 12 months ago. Importantly, we are not simply managing through the current cycle.

We are building a stronger platform designed to generate sustainable growth and profitability today while positioning loanDepot to capture significant upside when housing activity and mortgage demand eventually recover. Going forward, our priorities remain clear: grow market share, improve profitability, enhance shareholder value and continue building a differentiated homeownership platform that can succeed through all phases of the housing cycle. I'd like to thank Team loanDepot for their continued dedication and execution, as well as our customers, partners and shareholders for their ongoing support. The progress we've discussed today reflects the commitment of our people and the momentum we are building across the business. Thank you again for joining us.

We remain confident in our strategy, encouraged by our progress and look forward to updating you on our continued execution next quarter. Operator?

Operator: This concludes today's call. Thank you for attending. You may now disconnect.

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

anche nel settore giovanile sono presenti delle convocazioni per otto giovani giocatori: il programma

Oltre ai sette calciatori della Lazio convocati dalle rispettive Nazionali, anche nel settore giovanile sono presenti delle convocazioni per otto giovani giocatori: Galassi, Landi, Kovac, Eone', Miloiu, Elena, Bekirov, Nedev.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La destinazione di Jacopo Landi (Italia) e Emmanuel Eone' (Camerun) sarà il Qatar in occasione della Fifa World Cup U17.

L'Italia di Daniele Franceschini, nel gruplo E, giocherà con Giamaica, Costa D'Avorio e Uzbekistan. Il gruppo K è invece formato da Romania, Venezuela, Messico e Camerun.

Il difensore della Lazio era stato inizialemente convocato dalla rispettiva Nazionale, la Romania,  per il Mondiale U17. Partirà però con la Romania U20 e sarà a disposizione per l'Elite League.

Tre calciatori della Lazio classe 2010 saranno impegnati con le qualificazioni dell'Europeo Under 17. La Bulgaria ha convocato Bekirov e Nedev. L'Italia U17 ha chiamato invece Michele Elena che andrà in Armenia dove gli azzurrini sfideranno Lussemburgo, Francia e l'Armenia.

Ik difensore Kovac è stato convocato dalla Slovenia e sarà a disposizione per le qualificazioni dell'Europeo Under 19.

Chiamato dall'Argentina U20, Galassi prenderà parte al raduno della nazionale di categoria.

The Generation Essentials Group to Hold Extraordinary General Meeting on December 7, 2026

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

NEW YORK and PARIS and LONDON, Nov. 9, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- The Generation Essentials Group (NYSE: TGE; LSE: TGE) ("TGE" or the "Company"), today announced that it will hold an extraordinary general meeting of shareholders (the "EGM") at 9:00 a.m. (Eastern Standard Time) on December 7, 2026. The EGM will be held as a hybrid meeting with a physical meeting located at 66 Rue Jean-Jacques Rousseau, 75001 Paris, France and virtual attendance and participation by members and/or proxies via the internet at https://www.cstproxy.com/thegenerationessentials/2026.

A proposal to consolidate the Company's shares will be submitted to the Company's shareholders to be considered and voted upon at the EGM. If the proposal is approved by an ordinary resolution of the shareholders, (a) every fifteen (15) issued and unissued voting Class A ordinary shares of a par value of US$0.0000000264856557377049 each in the share capital of the Company will be consolidated into one (1) voting Class A ordinary share of a par value of US$0.0000003972848360655735 each, (b) every fifteen (15) issued and unissued voting Class B ordinary shares of a par value of US$0.0000000264856557377049 each will be consolidated into one (1) voting Class B ordinary share of a par value of US$0.0000003972848360655735 each, and (c) every fifteen (15) issued and unissued non-voting redeemable preferred shares of a par value of US$0.0000000264856557377049 each will be consolidated into one (1) non-voting redeemable preferred share of a par value of US$0.0000003972848360655735 each (collectively, the "Share Consolidation"), such that immediately following the Share Consolidation, the authorized share capital of the Company will be changed from US$50,000 divided into 1,887,814,313,346.23 shares of a par value of US$0.0000000264856557377049 each to US$50,000 divided into 125,854,287,556.42 shares of a par value of US$0.0000003972848360655735 each, comprising of (i) 119,403,256,791.2313 voting Class A ordinary shares, (ii) 4,854,429,177.56286 voting Class B ordinary shares and (iii) 1,596,601,587.62089 non-voting redeemable preferred shares (in each case, as rounded for presentation purposes only, or equivalent in fractions). In respect of any fractional entitlements to the issued consolidated shares resulting from the Share Consolidation, if so determined by the board of directors of the Company in its sole discretion, the directors will be authorized to settle any difficulty which arises in relation to the Share Consolidation as they consider expedient, including by capitalizing amounts standing to the credit of any reserve or fund of the Company and applying such sum in paying up unissued shares to round up any fractions of shares issued to or registered in the name of shareholders following or as a result of the Share Consolidation.

The Share Consolidation is intended to enable the Company to regain compliance with the New York Stock Exchange's continued listing standard relating to minimum average closing share price. There can be no assurance that the Share Consolidation, if approved and implemented, will result in a proportionate increase in the per-share price of the Company's ordinary shares, that any increased share price can be maintained, that the Share Consolidation will not adversely impact the market capitalization or liquidity of the Company's shares, or that the Company will successfully regain or maintain compliance with the NYSE's minimum bid price requirement or other continued listing standards.

The Board has fixed the close of business on November 9, 2026 (Eastern Standard Time) as the record date (the "Record Date") for determining the shareholders entitled to receive notice of, to attend, and to vote at, the EGM or any adjourned or postponed meeting thereof. Holders of record of the Company's Class A ordinary shares and Class B ordinary shares at the close of business on the Record Date are entitled to notice of, and to attend and vote at, the EGM or any adjournment or postponement thereof, in person at the meeting venue or virtually by means of electronic facilities. The notice of the EGM, which sets forth the resolution to be submitted for shareholder approval at the meeting, is available on the Company's website at https://www.thegenerationessentials.com/.

The Generation Essentials Group (NYSE: TGE; LSE: TGE), jointly established by AMTD Group, AMTD IDEA Group (NYSE: AMTD; SGX: HKB) and AMTD Digital Inc. (NYSE: HKD), is headquartered in France and focuses on global strategies and developments in multi-media, entertainment, and cultural affairs worldwide as well as hospitality and VIP services. TGE comprises L'Officiel, The Art Newspaper, movie and entertainment projects. Collectively, TGE is a diversified portfolio of media and entertainment businesses, and a global portfolio of premium properties. Also, TGE is a special purpose acquisition company (SPAC) sponsor manager, with its first SPAC successfully raised and priced on December 18, 2025.

Csquare Reports Third Quarter 2026 Results and Business Highlights

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

DALLAS, Nov. 9, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Csquare, Inc. (NYSE: CSQR) ("Csquare" or the "Company"), a leading provider of carrier-neutral data center solutions, today reported financial results for its fiscal third quarter ended September 30, 2026.

"Our third quarter results demonstrate strong and disciplined execution by our team and continued momentum across the business," said Spencer Mullee, Chief Executive Officer of Csquare. "We delivered strong revenue growth, record bookings and meaningful operating leverage while continuing to benefit from strong demand for our carrier-neutral digital infrastructure platform."

"These results reflect the solidity of our recurring revenue model and low net revenue churn, the strength of customer demand across our markets, and the operating leverage inherent in our business as Adjusted EBITDA margin expanded to 47.8% from 40.5% in the prior year period. Following our initial public offering in July, which resulted in $1.1 billion of debt reduction, our business is well positioned to continue executing on our strategy to deliver high-quality digital infrastructure across our urban core-focused portfolio."

"Demand for our offering remains strong and continues to accelerate. The strength of our platform is resonating in the market across traditional enterprise and higher density deployments as new and existing customers deploy and expand workloads on our platform. We believe our diversified portfolio, disciplined capital allocation strategy, and deep customer relationships position Csquare to continue capitalizing on a strong demand and pricing environment.  We remain focused on targeting high-quality customers, a diverse set of customers spanning multiple industries, and contracts that provide long-term, predictable cash flow."

RevenuesThird quarter total revenue increased 25.6% year-over-year to $302.3 million, compared to $240.8 million in the prior year period. Growth was driven by continued demand for the Company's colocation platform, customer deployments across existing and newly acquired facilities, and expansion of recurring infrastructure services. Revenue excluding metered power increased 22.1% to $276.2 million, underscoring the continued strength of the Company's recurring revenue base and customer expansion activity.

Commercial ActivityCsquare posted bookings of $66.1 million in the third quarter, representing the Company's 14th consecutive quarter of sequential bookings growth. The quarter was characterized by continued expansion of the commercial pipeline, strong pricing, and customers increasingly committing capacity further in advance of deployment requirements. Management believes these trends reflect ongoing supply constraints in many markets and growing customer focus on delivery certainty and long-term infrastructure planning.

Bookings were generated across enterprise customers, network providers, cloud-related deployments and higher-density infrastructure requirements. In addition, a significant portion of third-quarter bookings are expected to be deployed on shorter timelines than historical averages due to the utilization of existing capacity and targeted infrastructure upgrades rather than large-scale shell expansion projects.

Net Loss   Net loss for the third quarter of 2026 was $88.1 million, compared to a net loss of $29.8 million in the third quarter of 2025. The increase in net loss was primarily driven by higher year-over-year interest expense, IPO-related transaction costs, post-IPO stock-based compensation expenses, and costs associated with terminating certain pre-IPO compensation arrangements.

Adjusted EBITDAAdjusted EBITDA increased 44.3% year-over-year to $132.1 million, compared to $91.6 million in the third quarter of 2025. Adjusted EBITDA margin expanded to 47.8%, compared to 40.5% in the prior-year period, reflecting continued revenue growth, disciplined operating execution, and the inherent operating leverage of the Company's highly recurring business model.

A reconciliation of Adjusted EBITDA to Net Loss is included in the financial tables accompanying this release.

For the full year 2026, Csquare expects total revenues between $1.14 and $1.16 billion, Adjusted EBITDA between $490 and $500 million, recurring capital expenditures between $50 and $60 million, and non-recurring growth capital expenditures between $620 and $640 million.

The Company does not provide a reconciliation of forward-looking Adjusted EBITDA because certain items that affect the comparable GAAP measure cannot be reasonably provided without unreasonable effort.

IPO HighlightsDuring July 2026, Csquare successfully completed its initial public offering of 50.0 million shares of common stock at a public offering price of $21.00 per share and commenced trading on the New York Stock Exchange under the ticker symbol  NYSE: CSQR.

On July 27, 2026, the underwriters exercised their option to purchase an additional 7.499 million shares at the initial public offering price. In aggregate, the offering generated approximately $1.16 billion in net proceeds after underwriting discounts and commissions.

The IPO represents a significant milestone in the Company's evolution and strengthens its ability to execute its long-term growth strategy. As a public company, Csquare believes it is well positioned to benefit from enhanced access to capital markets, increased visibility among customers and investors, and greater strategic and financial flexibility.

The Company used the net proceeds to repay outstanding indebtedness, significantly reducing leverage and strengthening its balance sheet to support future investment opportunities.

Non-GAAP Financial MeasuresThis earnings release contains certain non-GAAP financial measures, including Adjusted EBITDA and FFO. Management believes these measures provide useful supplemental information regarding the Company's operating performance, and underlying business trends.

These measures should not be considered as alternatives to financial measures prepared in accordance with U.S. generally accepted accounting principles ("GAAP"). Reconciliations to the most directly comparable GAAP measures are included in the accompanying financial tables.

We prepare our financial statements in conformity with U.S. GAAP, though we believe evaluating our ongoing results of operations may be difficult if limited to reviewing only GAAP financial measures. Accordingly, we use non-GAAP financial measures to supplement our evaluation of our operations.

We believe that these non-GAAP financial measures, when taken collectively with our U.S. GAAP financial statements, may be helpful to investors because they allow for greater transparency into what measures we use in operating our business and measuring our performance and enable comparison of financial trends and results between periods where items may vary independent of business performance. These non-GAAP financial measures are presented for supplemental informational purposes only, should not be considered a substitute for financial information presented in accordance with GAAP, and may be different from similarly titled non-GAAP financial measures used by other companies. Because of these limitations, our non-GAAP financial measures should not be considered in isolation or as substitutes for net (loss) income, or any other measure calculated in accordance with U.S. GAAP, as applicable, and should be considered together with our GAAP financial measures and the reconciliations to the corresponding GAAP financial measures set forth in this press release.

Adjusted EBITDAWe define Adjusted EBITDA as net (loss) income, excluding (i) income taxes, (ii) interest expense, (iii) depreciation and amortization, (iv) gain on lease modification, (v) loss on extinguishment of debt, (vi) bargain purchase gain, (vii) stock-based compensation, (viii) other income (loss), net, and (ix) transaction and other costs. Transaction and other costs consist primarily of acquisition and integration costs, restructuring costs, costs associated with our initial public offering, and employee bonuses and loan extinguishment expenses directly attributable to specific transactions. The employee bonuses and loan extinguishment costs are recorded within Selling, marketing, general and administrative expenses in our Condensed Consolidated Statements of Operations. Management uses Adjusted EBITDA as a key measure of our operating performance and to assess the results of our business excluding certain items that we believe are not indicative of our core operating results. In addition, we believe Adjusted EBITDA is frequently used by securities analysts, investors, and other interested parties in the evaluation of data centers and other real estate companies. However, because Adjusted EBITDA is calculated before recurring cash charges, including interest expense and income taxes, which represent significant recurring cash charges necessary to operate our business, and is not adjusted for capital expenditures or other recurring cash requirements of our business, it should not be considered a measure of liquidity or an indicator of our cash flows and its utility as a measure of our performance is limited. Further, Adjusted EBITDA does not reflect our cash requirements or our ability to generate cash to meet those obligations. Other companies may calculate Adjusted EBITDA differently than we do and, as a result, Adjusted EBITDA may not be comparable to other companies' Adjusted EBITDA. Accordingly, Adjusted EBITDA should not be viewed in isolation or as a substitute for net (loss) income or any other performance measure calculated in accordance with U.S. GAAP.

Note: Adjusted EBITDA margin excludes metered power revenue.

Funds from OperationsManagement uses FFO, which is a non-GAAP financial measure commonly used in the real estate industry. This measure is used by management to evaluate performance corresponding to the retail colocation data center industry which has similarities to other real estate type companies. FFO is calculated in accordance with the standards approved by the Board of Governors of the National Association of Real Estate Investment Trusts. FFO represents net (loss) income (calculated in accordance with GAAP), excluding, when applicable (i) loss or gain from the disposition of real estate assets, (ii) depreciation and amortization and (iii) impairment write-downs of real estate assets and investments in entities when the impairment is directly attributable to decreases in the value of depreciable real estate held by the entity.

Management uses FFO as a supplemental performance measure because, in excluding the items identified in the calculation, it provides a performance measure that, when compared year over year, captures trends in utilization rates, pricing and operating costs. In addition, we believe FFO is frequently used by securities analysts, investors, and other interested parties in the evaluation of data centers and other real estate companies. However, because FFO excludes depreciation and amortization and does not capture the changes in the value of our data centers that result from use or market conditions, or the level of capital expenditures necessary to maintain the operating performance of our data centers, all of which have real economic effect and could materially impact our financial condition and results from operations, the utility of FFO as a measure of our performance is limited. Other companies may calculate FFO differently than we do and, as a result, FFO may not be comparable to other companies' FFO. Accordingly, FFO should not be considered in isolation or as a substitute for net (loss) income or any other performance measure calculated in accordance with U.S. GAAP.

Discussion of Non-GAAP Financial Measures

Adjusted EBITDA increased by $40.5 million, or 44.3%, to $132.1 million for the three months ended September 30, 2026, compared to $91.6 million for the three months ended September 30, 2025, and increased by $83.4 million, or 30.1%, to $360.7 million for the nine months ended September 30, 2026, compared to $277.3 million for the nine months ended September 30, 2025. This increase reflected continued improvement in operating performance across our platform, driven by growth in recurring colocation and other revenues, the impact of acquired sites, and operating leverage from our cost structure.

FFO decreased by $36.5 million, or 110%, to negative $3.2 million for the three months ended September 30, 2026, compared to $33.2 million for the three months ended September 30, 2025, and decreased by $56.2 million, or 50%, to $56.1 million for the nine months ended September 30, 2026, compared to $112.3 million for the nine months ended September 30, 2025. This decrease was primarily attributable to an increase in net loss, primarily driven by higher year-over-year interest expense resulting from the assumption of the 2021 ABS Notes in connection with the 2025 Portfolio Acquisition and interest incurred on additional ABS notes issued during the latter part of 2025, as well as from transaction and compensation costs related to our IPO. These impacts were partially offset by improved operating performance driven by growth in recurring revenue and gain on lease modification incurred during the second quarter.

Selected supplemental financial information:

Net favorable leasehold interest amortization(1)

Selected supplemental financial information:

Net favorable leasehold interest amortization(1)

(1) Net favorable/unfavorable leasehold interest amortization represents the periodic amortization of favorable and unfavorable lease terms associated with operating leasehold interests acquired in conjunction with business acquisitions. A favorable leasehold interest is recorded when the acquired lease terms are below current market rates. Conversely, an unfavorable leasehold interest is recorded when the acquired lease terms are above current market rates. These amounts are amortized on a straight-line basis over the remaining lease term, including renewal periods that are reasonably certain to be exercised. For operating leases, the favorable and unfavorable lease terms are recognized as a component of operating lease cost and presented within cost of revenues, excluding depreciation and amortization in the Consolidated Statements of Operations.

(2) Transaction and other costs include cash incentive compensation and loan extinguishment costs associated with the settlement of legacy pre-IPO incentive compensation arrangements in connection with the IPO. The cash incentive compensation and loan extinguishment costs associated with the settlement of legacy pre-IPO incentive compensation arrangements totaled $25.3 million and $33.6 million for the three and nine months ended September 30, 2026, respectively, and were recorded within Selling, marketing, general and administrative expenses in our unaudited condensed consolidated statements of operations. No such costs were recorded during the three and nine months ended September 30, 2025.

(3) Recurring capital expenditures represent capital expenditures associated with maintaining and extending the useful lives of data centers and other assets that support current revenues.

Quantum Computing Inc. Reports Third Quarter 2026 Financial Results

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Third-quarter revenue of $9.5 million exceeds revenue generated during the entire first half of 2026

Acquired photonics and advanced packaging businesses contributed to commercial revenue growth while supporting QCi's quantum technology roadmap

Ends quarter with approximately $1.3 billion in cash, cash equivalents and investments

HOBOKEN, N.J., Nov. 9, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Quantum Computing Inc. ("QCi" or the "Company") (Nasdaq: QUBT), a vertically integrated quantum company pioneering photonics and semiconductor manufacturing, today announced financial results for the third quarter ended September 30, 2026.

QCi generated $9.5 million in revenue during the third quarter, exceeding the $9.2 million reported for the entire first half of 2026. Revenue growth was primarily driven by the Luminar Semiconductors, Inc. ("LSI"), NuCrypt, LLC ("NuCrypt") and NHanced Semiconductors, Inc. ("NHanced") businesses acquired during the first half of 2026, including acquired photonic components, quantum communications products and advanced packaging services sold to the aerospace, government and industrial markets. The Company continues to focus on expanding commercial relationships, integrating its manufacturing capabilities and advancing its proprietary quantum technologies as it moves into the next phase of its development.

"For several years, we've been building the technology and capabilities to make quantum more practical and accessible," said Dr. Yuping Huang, Chief Executive Officer of QCi. "We're starting to see those investments come together, and our focus is increasingly shifting from what we can build to what we can put to work for customers. We're encouraged by our progress, but recognize there's considerable work ahead. Our priorities are clear: growing our commercial business, scaling manufacturing and advancing our quantum roadmap. I've always believed that being early creates opportunity. Execution is what makes that opportunity last."

Quantum Computing Inc. (Nasdaq: QUBT) is an integrated quantum photonics company developing and manufacturing technologies that use light to compute complex information, capture hard-to-detect signals and communicate more securely. QCi brings together quantum optics, integrated photonics, semiconductor manufacturing and advanced packaging to create a more direct path from advanced science to practical products.

QCi's portfolio includes quantum machines, photonic chips, components, modules and subsystems, as well as foundry and advanced manufacturing services. Its technologies are designed to operate at room temperature and low power, without the specialized cooling infrastructure required by many quantum approaches. These advantages enable deployment across computing and AI, secure communications, sensing and imaging, aerospace and defense, and other demanding applications.

Headquartered in Hoboken, New Jersey, QCi has operations across the United States. By combining deep physics, device engineering and scalable manufacturing, QCi is working to make quantum and advanced photonic technologies more accessible and useful in the real world.

To learn more about QCi and its approach to harnessing light for practical quantum computing, please visit www.quantumcomputinginc.com.

A key report for where inflation stood in October comes out Tuesday. Here's what to expect

📅 2026-11-09  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Falling gasoline prices provided some relief to consumers in October, but Federal Reserve officials are increasingly concerned that inflation pressures elsewhere in the economy are proving harder to contain.

Prices at the pump declined by more than 4%, according to the Energy Information Administration, providing a respite from a surge that had added more than a dollar a gallon over the past year.

However, the consumer price index for the month, which the Bureau of Labor Statistics will release Tuesday at 8:30 a.m., is expected to show only a slight let-up.

Meanwhile, Fed officials say they are worried that inflation initially driven higher by President Donald Trump's tariffs and the Iran war is proving more persistent than expected, with price pressures emerging in sectors beyond those directly affected by the shocks.

At a time when the central bank is increasingly confident about the state of the labor market, concerns over the cost of living are what are top of mind for the Fed.

"We've been moving up to what historically we'd consider full employment, and the big problem has all been on the inflation side. We go around, we talk to people everywhere in this district, all we hear about is affordability on the business side, the cost," Chicago Fed President Austan Goolsbee said Monday during an event in his home district. "So we've got to come to terms with this cost-side and inflation-side failure."

The CPI reading for all items is expected to show an increase of 0.3%. Core inflation, which excludes volatile food and energy costs, is forecast to increase 0.2%, according to the Dow Jones consensus forecast. While both would be slower than the respective September monthly gains of 0.6% and 0.3%, they still represent further price pressures.

There will be no annual reading for October because of the government shutdown a year ago, which prevented the BLS from collecting some data and compiling a full index level. Because of the data issue, there isn't a comparable figure for the index.

Bank of America, using an assumed baseline interpolating data for the period last year, estimated the annual growth rates at 3.8% for headline and 2.7% for core, both 0.1 percentage point higher than September and well above the Fed's 2% goal.

Goolsbee said the current run on inflation is defying what normally could be expected from supply shocks and tariffs, with the current situation intensified by the hyperscaler build-out related to artificial intelligence.

"It was supposed to have gone away by now, and we're now starting to see the inflation kicking up in areas that probably weren't from oil prices and tariffs, like a bunch of services. Now this stuff with the AI data center build-out is, to my eyes, starting to look more just like old-fashioned demand overheating," he said.

The massive investment in data centers is creating demand for construction workers, electricity and equipment, potentially adding to price pressures even as AI promises longer-term productivity gains, he added.

Though the Fed in October voted to hold its benchmark interest rate in place, markets are pricing in a better than 80% probability that the central bank will raise rates by a quarter percentage point at its next meeting in December, according to CME FedWatch data.

Goolsbee, who doesn't vote on the rate-setting Federal Open Market Committee this year but will in 2027, didn't commit to which way he was leaning on additional rate hikes, though he said current conditions could warrant action.

"The conventional wisdom there is: Be aggressive up front, to never let the economy overheat, because once it gets overheated, it's extremely painful to get rid of that kind of overheating," he said. "So I think we've got to watch out in that space."

The CPI release comes with economic growth strong — 3.4% in the third quarter for gross domestic product and tracking at 3.5% for the final three months of the year, according to the Atlanta Fed — and the labor market seemingly in solid shape as well. Nonfarm payrolls expanded by a much better-than-expected 165,000 in October, while the unemployment rate edged lower to 4.1%, though inflation-adjusted wage growth has been negative.

That puts the inflation reading squarely in focus.

Bank of America said it sees tariff impacts fading but that goods prices could be pushed higher because of AI demand. Further, the firm is cautious about "sticky" services prices as well as pressures from health insurance and autos.

The bank has been hawkish in terms of Fed expectations and sees a December hike as likely so long as there isn't an escalated stock market sell-off that might dissuade the central bank.

"This will be the final inflation report before the Fed's December 8-9 meeting. If our forecast is correct, we think it would effectively lock in another 25bp hike, all else equal," BofA economist Stephen Juneau said in a client note. "Absent an indisputably soft 0.1% [month-over-month] print, we doubt the inflation data will dissuade the Fed from hiking in December. The bigger risk to our call comes from financial markets."

SCOTUS allows Trump admin to temporarily resume border construction in Texas' Big Bend

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Supreme Court delivers major immigration wins for President Donald Trump, curtailing asylum claims and temporary protected status. Laura Ingraham exposes how activist judges appointed under Democratic administrations have historically blocked border enforcement efforts. The segment highlights the dramatic expansion of TPS under Biden and tragic cases of criminal illegal aliens, arguing for stronger border security to prevent more American fatalities.

The U. S. Supreme Court on Monday cleared the way for the Trump administration to resume border wall construction in West Texas, granting an emergency request from the federal government to lift a lower court's hold on the project.

The justices' ruling stays a preliminary injunction issued last month by a federal district judge in El Paso, who had temporarily halted border wall construction in the region designated by U. S. Customs and Border Protection as the Big Bend Sector while litigation proceeds.

Justice Ketanji Brown Jackson indicated she would have denied the stay request but did not file a written dissent.

LIBERAL JUSTICE HAMMERS SUPREME COURT FOR FAST-TRACKING EMERGENCY DOCKET CASES

A construction vehicle builds the border wall near Sunland Park, N.M. in 2026. The U.S. Supreme Court on Monday granted the Trump administration’s request to lift an injunction blocking construction of the border wall along the remote Big Bend region of Texas. (Christian Torres/Getty Images)

The lawsuit against the project was brought by a coalition of environmental groups and local residents, who claim the Department of Homeland Security violated constitutional provisions by proceeding without congressional authorization and bypassing environmental regulations.

Spanning more than 500 miles, the Big Bend Sector covers roughly a quarter of the U. S.–Mexico border, though it accounted for just 1. 3% of all border apprehensions in 2025, Reuters reported.

MULLIN TELLS CONGRESS PRIMARY BORDER WALL WILL BE DONE BY JUNE 2027, SECONDARY WALL BY SUMMER 2028

A drone view of a new section of border wall being constructed in Arizona. (REUTERS/Cheney Orr)

Administration officials have argued the wall is vital to stopping cartel operations active south of the border and curbing the smuggling of large quantities of illicit drugs into the U.S.

Most of the proposed construction in the Big Bend Sector will take place on privately owned land, according to administration officials.

Big Bend National Park is slated for a 17-mile barrier, though officials noted it will not feature the 30-foot steel pedestrian fencing planned in other areas along the southern border.

President Donald Trump has long touted border wall expansion as a signature initiative of his strategy to seal the southern border and crack down on illegal immigration.

Are the James Webb Space Telescope's mysterious Little Red Dots actually tiny black holes left over from the Big Bang era?

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

"The door was open on this theory — that Little Red Dots could be primordial black holes. We've now cracked it open a little farther."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Ever since NASA's James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) started observing the early universe, it has been providing almost as many surprises as it has answers. One such surprise are the so-called Little Red Dots, cosmic objects that seemingly disappear completely before the universe is two billion years old.

New research suggests that Little Red Dots may actually be connected primordial black holes, tiny black holes born not from dying stars but rather from fluctuations in the density of matter just after the Big Bang, in the earliest moments of cosmic history. Should they exist, primordial black holes would predate stars, galactic dust particles, and elements heavier than hydrogen and helium. Now, researchers have used JWST data to test whether one particular Little Red Dot, known as blackAbell 2744-QSO1 (QSO1), could host a primordial black hole.

"A black hole is an object born from the gravitational death of a star. Primordial black holes are a very different story," study team member Valerio De Luca, of the Department of Physics and Astronomy at Johns Hopkins University (JHU) in Maryland, said in a statement.

"They come from the physics of the early universe that predates the first stars, when the universe was expanding exponentially in time and space. So the mechanisms for creating primordial black holes are different," De Luca added.

Though scientists have theorized about the existence of primordial black holes since they were first proposed by Stephen Hawking in the 1970s, no hard evidence has presented itself. One of the issues scientists have puzzled over is how to tell the difference between a primordial black hole and a "traditional" or astrophysical black hole formed from the supernova death and collapse of a massive star.

"Little Red Dots could surround astrophysical black holes, and most probably many of them do, but they could also be primordial black holes, if primordial black holes exist," De Luca said. "So the question becomes, Which ones are primordial and how can we tell?"

De Luca and colleagues turned their attention to QS01, located around 13.1 billion light-years from Earth, which is seen as it was just 700 million years after the Big Bang. What makes this Little Red Dot stand out is the fact that it is almost entirely composed of hydrogen and helium. Because these were the universe's first elements, it could be more unspoiled and primordial than other Little Red Dots.

The team modeled how a Little Red Dot like QS01 could form from a primordial black hole. That was necessary because the circumstances under which this could happen would have to be very specific.

"There are constraints on primordial black holes from cosmological observations," said team member Loris Del Grosso, also of JHU's Department of Physics and Astronomy. "There can only be so many primordial black holes in the universe, for example, and there cannot be too many of them in a given cluster."

Considering three possible scenarios, the team found that the most likely situation is a smaller primordial black hole consuming surrounding gas and energy until it grew to be the size of QSO1.

"The door was open on this theory — that Little Red Dots could be primordial black holes," Del Grosso said. "We've now cracked it open a little farther by showing some scenarios are more likely than others."

Del Grosso and crew now intend to look at the other 100 or so Little Red Dots seen by the JWST to determine how many of them could have started life as primordial black holes.

Then scientists may finally have the evidence to say these long-theorized ancient black holes actually existed.

"We don't have a clear understanding of what happens in the early universe right after the Big Bang," De Luca concluded. "If primordial black holes do exist, then something had to happen to make them in the first place, which would be a groundbreaking discovery in physics."

The team's findings were published in June in the journal Physical Review Letters.

Robert Lea is a science journalist in the U.K. whose articles have been published in Physics World, New Scientist, Astronomy Magazine, All About Space, Newsweek and ZME Science. He also writes about science communication for Elsevier and the European Journal of Physics. Rob holds a bachelor of science degree in physics and astronomy from the U.K.’s Open University. Follow him on Twitter @sciencef1rst.

Should you leave Australia to avoid paying 47% tax on crypto gains?

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Some Australian crypto investors are moving overseas to avoid the huge CGT increase from July 2027. But there are also less drastic ways you can pay less tax.

In part one of our special on how to avoid Australia’s massive hike in capital gains taxes on crypto in July next year, we looked at borrowing against your Bitcoin or setting up a self-managed super fund (SMSF), among four ways to mitigate the impact of the taxes.While an SMSF offers Australians the greatest tax advantages, it’s only suitable for older investors, or those who are willing to lock up their crypto for decades until they retire. For younger investors, moving overseas could reduce your future crypto tax bills to 0% — but there are less drastic options to save on tax too.

Australia’s impending tax changes have already inspired crypto king Fred Schebesta, founder of Finder.com, to become a tax exile in Singapore.

From July, the top rate on real, long-term crypto gains will increase to 47%, minus the effects of inflation on the cost basis.

For upwardly mobile, single crypto investors, the “nuclear option” of leaving the country to avoid giving almost half their gains to the government will look increasingly attractive.Many of the world’s top crypto hubs — Singapore, Portugal, Malta, UAE and Hong Kong — offer 0% capital gains taxes on long-term crypto gains in an attempt to attract wealthy investors.

Also read: The one thing these 6 global crypto hubs all have in common...Should you leave Australia to avoid paying 47% tax on crypto gains?

Ben Simpson, founder of crypto advisory service Collective Shift, says some of his members have already pulled the trigger.

“Yeah, we’ve had a number of people that have sort of packed up and gone basically, ‘f—- this. I’m off to the Bahamas or whatnot,’” he says.

Andrea Yuen, CEO of Sywftx, says she is keenly aware of the tax advantages of living outside of Australia. “I lived in Singapore for 30 years. I do miss my no capital gains tax,” she says. But she didn’t move there for tax reasons, and cautions that uprooting your life to avoid a bit of tax is a pretty drastic step.

“I would never say that we should structure our lives only to think about tax outcomes,” she says. “There are many other wonderful reasons why I’ve come back to Australia and continued to live in Australia.”

Simpson has considered the idea, given he already spends half the year in America looking after Collective Shift US, but looking into the rules put him off. “You really have to cut pretty much all ties to Australia. So if you are genuinely happy to leave and don’t want to come back for the majority of the year, and cut your healthcare and cut all your ties back to Australia, then that is a strategy.”

“Bitcoin Jesus” Roger Ver gave up his US residency in 2014, and ten years later he was arrested and charged over failing to correctly report the value of his crypto holdings and paying exit taxes. He was forced to pay almost $50 million to make the case go away. Similarly, Australians leaving the country face a hefty exit tax on departure, which may require selling a decent amount of your stack.

“They tax you on the way out. If you’ve got a lot of gains or profits you’re sitting on, they’ll catch you when you leave,” says Adrian Forza, director of Crypto Tax Australia. “Arguably it’d be bigger in the future, but it’s still difficult to stomach selling off everything to leave.”

Forza explains you also have the option of paying the Australian taxes later on, but on the full value of the gain after you sell the asset. So if you expect Bitcoin to go up, you’d be best off paying the exit tax now.

Related: Australia releases draft bill to adopt OECD crypto tax reporting framework

Simpson is only 27 and the prospect of locking up his crypto portfolio for forty years holds little appeal. Instead, he trades and holds crypto via a company to benefit from the 25% company tax rate.

Running a single-person company is easier than you might think, but not worth the hassle unless you have a reasonable amount of capital and intend to make a lot of trades. It requires registering the company with ASIC and paying an annual fee, paying an accountant to produce financial statements and/or an annual company tax return and mucking about with payroll software and payday superannuation contributions if you plan to pay yourself an income. You can pay yourself a dividend at any time when you need some money.

“The company structure is good if you’re wanting to cap the capital gains tax at the company tax rate,” he says, but notes there’s a big catch: when you take funds out of the company, you need to pay the difference between the 25% company tax rate and your personal tax rate. “You still need to pay your marginal income tax rates. If you’re on the top tax rate, like 47%,” he says.

You may be wondering: if you have to pay your income tax rate anyway, which will equal the CGT rate from July, what is the benefit in paying a few thousand dollars and filling out a bunch of paperwork to run a company?

Simpson explains that the key advantage is that while you are building up your crypto portfolio by trading in and out of different coins, you only pay the 25% company rate for each sale. That means paying up to 22% less than CGT each time you exit a position, which builds into an even more significant saving over time.

“Instead of paying 47% because you’ve just taken the profits… then the CGT amount is only 25% — the company tax rate. So when you put that money back in, you’ve got more capital to play with.”

However, if you don’t trade enough, it might be taxed at 30%.

Forza says the advantages are greater for those on higher incomes.

It’s not a particularly useful structure if you just plan to just hold your Bitcoin, however. There are some potential advantages but arguably not enough of them to be worth the hassle.

The 50% CGT discount has been replaced by an indexation discount, which takes into account how much inflation affects how much you paid originally for an asset.

This means that investors who buy and hold dividend stocks or index funds for a long period will receive a bigger CGT discount. So should you drop crypto and buy a bunch of ETFs so you can pay less tax?

The short answer is no: the new CGT ‘discount’ is largely an illusion, as it simply measures your real gain after inflation.

Simpson says crypto has been hit hard by the CGT changes, but it still offers the best risk-reward opportunity.

History suggests that despite volatility and years-long bear markets, the return from investing in Bitcoin over time would still leave investors ahead of ETF investors even after paying more tax. Investing further down the risk curve with altcoins however, can either pay off handsomely or end in total disaster.

JPMorgan assumes that Bitcoin will compound by 10% on average in the years ahead. (Source JPMorgan/Bitcoin Archive)

Simpson says he convinced his younger sister, who is a basketball player on a low income, to invest in Bitcoin two years ago rather than putting money into a savings account. “She’s up like 40% or 50% in her portfolio and she’s like, ‘wow, this is going to get me so much further towards buying a house one day than sitting in cash and losing 10% [to inflation over time],” he says.

“That’s why I think Bitcoin makes so much sense to accumulate. And the tax considerations are not really there for most people I think.”

Yuen says crypto is one of the few asset classes that offers investors the chance to make double-digit returns in a few months, or to potentially double their money. Even after paying the new taxes, crypto investors with those sorts of returns will still be ahead.

“My dad always said to me, it’s a good thing, if you’ve made profits, you should be happy to pay some tax on it.”

Well maybe not happy about it, but you’ll still be making money.

5 Nintendo Switch 2 features everyone should know about

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Switch 2 has lots of little tricks to discover: some inherited from the original Switch and some new.

Chances are that you aren't taking full advantage of everything your Nintendo Switch 2 can do. It's easy to jump right into games and ignore the various settings that help your system run at its best.

The Switch 2 is a pretty direct upgrade to the original Switch — its design, OS, controllers and other aspects are refinements rather than something entirely new. That means many of the Switch 1's tips and tricks work on the newer console too, so if you're a Switch veteran you might already know where to look. But there are other easy-to-miss improvements to find, and they're unique to the newer system.

Whether it's making controls more comfortable or repurposing hardware you already have, you should know about the Switch 2's neat little features. For instance, you might have overlooked the option to share games with friends, which can save you all money. Try these features out before you jump into a game on your next session.

The Joy-Con 2 controllers are larger than the original models but still small enough to sink between the couch cushions or lose sight of underneath a blanket. Next time you can't find your controllers, your console can help. Open the Controllers page from the main menu, then hit Search for Controllers.

All compatible controllers you've paired to the system will appear; to find one, highlight it and hold L or R (or press the touchscreen). While you're holding the button, the controller will vibrate and play a sound, helping you track it down. If you can't find one, try walking around with the console to get back in range of it.

There are a few caveats here. While you can pair original Switch controllers with a Switch 2, this feature can't locate them. Third-party Switch controllers may or may not work. You can't ping a dead controller with this, and it won't find a controller that's paired with another console.

Most laptops, phones and similar devices now include a feature to cap their charging at 80 percent (or similar values). This is designed to protect the health of the device's battery over several years, since keeping lithium-ion batteries at 100 percent for extended periods wears them faster than lower percentages.

Your Switch 2 can also do this: open Settings and scroll to System. About halfway down, you'll see a toggle titled Stop Charging Around 80-90%. Turn this on, and your system will limit itself when it's on the dock or otherwise charging. If you're headed on a long trip, feel free to shut this off first to fully juice up. But in general, especially if your Switch 2 sits on the dock most of the time, this is worth having on.

Speaking of charging, the System Battery (%) option next to this is appreciated, too. It will show the exact battery level instead of a general symbol.

While many modern games have expansive control scheme options, sometimes you can't adjust exactly what you'd like. For those cases, you're able to remap buttons at the system level. Open Settings and go to Accessibility. At the top, choose Button Mapping — if you plan to use this often, enable Button Mapping in Quick Settings and a shortcut to this tool will appear when you hold the Home button.

In this menu, you'll see each connected controller on the left side. Select one and choose Edit Controller's Mappings to show its buttons, where you're free to remap most of the inputs to anything else. For example, you could swap the ZR and ZL buttons with R and L, or move an inconvenient input to a button that's in closer reach.

The C button is of particular interest; if you don't use GameChat, you can disable the button to avoid accidental activation. Alternatively, turn it into a secondary input for another button, like a D-pad direction or pressing one of the sticks. Check the Control Stick Settings if you'd like to swap the left and right sticks or use a Joy-Con 2 vertically when a game intends you to hold it horizontally.

While you can't apply a remap to a certain game, you can save up to five profiles per controller and swap them as needed. After hitting Save, choose Save as a Preset and give it a name. When remapping is applied, you'll see a blue wrench icon at the bottom-left of the menu and anywhere else that controller icon appears. Games are not aware of remapping done this way, so if you've swapped ZR and R, for example, you'll need to press the latter when the game prompts the former.

There's one more related option at the bottom of Settings > Controllers & Accessories: Swap A and B. This simple toggle does what it says and is great if you're used to PlayStation or Xbox controllers that have the Confirm button at the bottom and Cancel on the right.

You have options for letting others enjoy your games, thanks to Nintendo's virtual game cards. Any game loaded onto your system is playable by all other users on the console under their own profile. If you have a Nintendo family group set up, other members can borrow your games on their system for two weeks (during which time you're unable to play them).

Open the Virtual Game Cards page on the Home screen to see your collection. Choose one and hit Lend to Family Group Member, then Select a User to Lend To. To do this, you have to be physically near the other person. You can only loan three games at a time, and a borrower can only have a single game "checked out" at once. Either party can return the game early, and you're free to loan it again if desired after 14 days.

There's also the GameShare feature, where you can stream a copy of compatible games so anyone nearby with a Switch or Switch 2 can join you in multiplayer. You can also do this online using GameChat for friends with a Switch 2. Only a few titles (including Donkey Kong Bananza, Clubhouse Games and Super Mario Odyssey) are supported, but it's worth a look for a low-cost multiplayer.

There are a few ways to reduce slowdowns across your Switch 2 so you don't get bogged down in menus all the time. If you're the only user on your system, it's a waste of time to select your profile every time you open a game or the eShop. To avoid this, go to Settings > Users and enable Skip Selection Screen. This option won't appear if your system has multiple users.

You might also be able to minimize loading times in Switch 1 games. These can see smoother performance when playing off the dock if you visit Settings > System > Nintendo Switch Software Handling > Handheld Mode Boost. By enabling this, your Switch 2 runs original Switch titles as if they were in TV mode, which can improve their graphics or performance. As a trade-off, it will likely use more battery, and there are some control quirks (like the touchscreen not working). There's also an option to add this to Quick Settings for easier toggling.

Finally, there's a much better way to share media you create on your Switch 2. To share your screenshots and video clips much faster, open the Gallery icon on the home screen, hit the Settings cog on the left, and pick your profile. Enable Automatic Uploads and your system will upload up to 100 items, held for 30 days. Then on your phone, install the Nintendo Switch app and sign in with your Nintendo account. By tapping the same Album icon in the app, you're able to access your uploads and share them over chat apps, social media or similar.
