Canton Hosts Tokenized US Share Trade Under SEC Innovation Exemption

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: A tokenized share transaction involving a Nasdaq-listed company has taken place on Canton under a US regulatory exemption, according to an announcement from venue operator TSV. The November 4 transaction involved shares of Canton Strategic Holdings and a permissioned pool using Tradecraft's automated market maker technology.

A tokenized share transaction involving a Nasdaq-listed company has taken place on Canton under a US regulatory exemption, according to an announcement from venue operator TSV. The November 4 transaction involved shares of Canton Strategic Holdings and a permissioned pool using Tradecraft's automated market maker technology. The important development is not that a stock price was mirrored on a blockchain, but that the transaction was structured around the issuer, a transfer agent and verified holders.

TSV says DRW Cumberland purchased tokenized Canton Strategic Holdings common stock from existing shareholder Five North. Fairmint, an SEC-registered transfer agent, recorded the shares in token form in a verified wallet, and Flowdesk provided liquidity. The parties describe this as the first trade of its kind under the SEC's innovation exemption. Because this is an operator announcement, the scope of that first-of-kind claim should be attributed to the participants. What can be identified concretely is the named issuer, the parties, the permissioned environment and the transfer-agent role in the share record.

Tokenized equities can describe several very different products. Some are economic exposures issued by a separate intermediary; others aim to represent the issuer's shares with the rights and recordkeeping attached to conventional stock. Here the issuer-sponsored structure and transfer-agent involvement are central. They are intended to connect token holdings with the legally recognized ownership register, rather than just a derivative tracking a market price. Investors still need to know transfer restrictions, voting rights, eligibility rules and how corporate actions are administered. A blockchain token is not proof of all those protections by itself.

The reported transactions occurred in a permissioned pool with verified participants. That is fundamentally different from announcing that any wallet can buy US shares without checks or through a decentralized exchange. Restricted access may be a design choice needed to satisfy securities obligations, investor eligibility and transfer controls. It may also limit liquidity and interoperability compared with the open token markets crypto users are accustomed to. The test of such infrastructure is whether these controls can function at scale without making trading and settlement more cumbersome than conventional systems.

The company referred to an SEC innovation exemption announced in September. The transaction should be read within the specific conditions of the applicable relief, not as proof the SEC has deregulated all tokenized securities. Different venues, issuers or instruments may require their own legal analysis. Reporting on this area is especially sensitive to the distinction between an operational pilot, a live limited transaction and a broadly available retail market. This appears to be the second category: a real transaction, but with explicit participation boundaries.

Future announcements should reveal the number and variety of issuers, whether transactions settle consistently, how ownership and voting are handled, and whether participants can transfer positions between approved venues. Costs, order-book depth and operational incidents will be more revealing than another first-trade headline. If tokenized US equities become a durable market structure, those unglamorous details will matter at least as much as the chain carrying the record.

Primary source: Tradecraft / TSV official announcement. For additional context, see NewsBTC’s earlier report on transfer agents supporting tokenized securities.

Children’s Place files for bankruptcy protection, seeks buyer

📅 2026-11-09  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Children's clothing retailer The Children's Place has filed for chapter 11 bankruptcy protection, it said Sunday.

The company is looking to sell substantially all of its assets and is seeking offers from potential buyers by Dec. 9, with a goal to close a deal by the end of December.

The retailer, whose brands include The Children's Place and Gymboree, says it operates two digital storefronts and 514 North American stores, and has distribution in 13 countries through franchise and wholesale partners. The company's performance has taken a hit in recent years from tariff hikes and slower consumer spending, among other factors.

Its stores and e-commerce sites remain open for now, and the company is planning to continue its ongoing inventory sales with court approval.

"For nearly 60 years, The Children's Place has supported growing families with apparel that customers have come to know and love. We recognize the significance of this development for our employees, customers, vendors, and other partners, and we are committed to conducting an efficient and transparent sale process," said Rudy Morando, co-chief restructuring officer of The Children's Place. "In the meantime, we remain focused on serving the families who have trusted The Children's Place for generations."

This move comes days after interim Children's Place CEO Muhammad Asif Seemab and Chief Strategy Officer Muhammad Umair stepped down from their roles. Seemab and Umair will both remain on the company's board. CFO John Szczepanski took on the role of acting president and principal executive officer.

Seemab is also managing director of Saudi-based Mithaq Capital, which took a majority stake in The Children's Place in 2024 when the company was facing financial trouble.

Even with support from the investors, the company has not been able to significantly improve its financial outlook. In its bankruptcy filing, the company reported about $742 million in assets, compared to about $881 million in debts.

In the second fiscal quarter of 2026, its most recently reported quarter, the company's net sales dropped nearly 19% from the same period the year prior. At the time, Seemab said in a news release that during the quarter the company was "stabilizing our customer file" and "driving traffic to our stores and websites," and saw "lower conversion, combined with a very clearance-heavy mix."

He also pointed to tough comparisons to the prior-year period, due in part to back-to-school shopping demand that "was uniquely elevated ... ahead of anticipated tariff price increases."

The Children's Place stock has plummeted in recent years and was trading around 18 cents per share on Monday.

Apple starts offering $50 extended usage packs for AI features in Creator Studio

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It’ll cost you to access Logic Pro’s AI assistant after you reach the monthly usage limit.

Apple has made it clear for a while that Siri AI is bound by daily usage limits and that it will offer expanded access via iCloud+ plans. The company hasn't shed more light on exactly how that will work, but it has revealed how Creator Studio users can continue wielding the AI features of those apps after they reach the regular limits.

It has started selling $50 intelligence usage packs for Creator Studio, and Logic Pro is the first app to support these. Apple updated Logic Pro last month to include an assistant that's powered by its own models as well as those from third parties. The company said the chatbot can help "creators and producers navigate production workflows, start a new song, set up a vocal track from a single prompt or refine an active project."

As noted by 9to5Mac, folks can view their AI usage in the app by going to Logic Pro on the menu bar, then Intelligence Features > Show Usage. The window that pops up includes a Buy More Usage button — clicking this takes you to the option to buy an intelligence usage pack.

A support page explains that users with active Apple Creator Studio subscriptions in the US, UK, Canada, Brazil, France, Germany, India and Mexico can buy intelligence usage packs to keep using AI features after they reach their monthly usage limit. A pack is tied to the Apple Account used to purchase it — there's no Family Sharing support.

"Unused additional usage purchased with intelligence usage packs roll over from month to month and expire 12 months after purchase," Apple added. "Additional usage gets consumed for relevant features only after you've reached your monthly limit in any given month."

It seems likely that Apple will expand intelligence usage packs to other Creator Studio apps, such as Final Cut Pro and Pixelmator Pro. It's interesting that Apple is opting for this approach over a regular subscription, but perhaps the company has other offerings planned for the future. For what it's worth, Creator Studio apps are available via a subscription or one-time purchase.

Cisco adds Anthropic’s Claude Mythos to AI Defense for red teaming

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Customers can't prompt Mythos directly; it only tests systems they own and have registered.

Cisco is adding Anthropic’s Claude Mythos to its AI Defense red-teaming tool.

Cisco will add Anthropic’s Claude Mythos to AI Defense to plan attacks on customers’ own AI apps and agents, a practice called red teaming. Customers get no open prompt access, every test is logged to a named person, and it comes first to select enterprises.

Cisco will add Anthropic’s Claude Mythos to AI Defense, the company announced at its Cisco Live event in Melbourne. AI Defense attacks companies’ own AI systems to find weak spots before hackers do. Mythos will come first to select enterprise customers, with no date set.

Mythos will plan tests and come up with attack ideas against AI apps and agents, a practice known as red teaming. In July, Cisco researchers used it to push 15 chatbots past their guardrails.

Customers cannot type prompts to Mythos directly. It works only on a fixed job, against systems the customer owns and has registered for testing.

Every test is tied to a named person, recorded for audit and can be stopped at any time. Mythos cannot scan the wider internet, Cisco said in a blog post.

“Enterprises are increasingly building AI agents into their core operations and want to test those agents thoroughly before putting them into production,” said Michael Moore, Anthropic’s cybersecurity lead.

Security firms are lining up to build Mythos into their products. Palo Alto Networks launched always-on testing on Mythos and OpenAI’s cyber model in September. IBM said on Monday it plans to add Mythos to its Concert Protect tool in early 2027.

That suits Anthropic, which puts Mythos inside partners’ products rather than handing out the model. It released Mythos 5.2 on Monday, still only for vetted organisations. Tight controls matter: in April, an unauthorised group reached Mythos through a contractor’s access.

Cisco also updated its Splunk tools that track what AI agents cost and grade the quality of their work. A cost tool called Tokenomics enters private preview in December.

Fervo now has $12B in orders for its geothermal power tech

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Geothermal company Fervo couldn’t have picked a better time to deliver its first power plants.

In its third quarter earnings report, released Monday, the company said it now has $11.9 billion worth of contracted revenue on the books, up from $7.2 billion last quarter.

Fervo has been hitting its deadlines recently, which undoubtedly has helped to drive orders.

As hyperscalers scour the earth for electricity to power their AI data centers, Fervo is on track to complete a 396 megawatt power plant for Google by the end of 2028. The geothermal company connected its first 100 megawatt power plant to the grid earlier this year.

Both projects are built at Cape Station in Utah, a resource that could generate 8.4 gigawatts of electricity. Previously, experts told Fervo the site could only produce about 4 gigawatts.

The Google project, now called Cape Google 400, will be grid connected, co-founder and CEO Tim Latimer said in an earnings call Monday. The arrangement means that Google can draw power from the deal and sell it to other customers if it’s not ready to use it.

“We build Cape Station on our schedule. Google can develop a potential data center on their schedule, and neither party is waiting on the other,” he said.

Latimer’s statement also underlines an advantage that geothermal has over competing generating sources like large nuclear or natural gas power plants — the phased nature of its construction.

Fervo newer wells are deeper and hotter, each capable of generating 15 megawatts gross power, and several are linked together into what the company calls a GeoBlock. The first completed GeoBlock generated 33 megawatts, while recent ones hit 50 megawatts. They’re sized to match the demand of a typical data center hall, so hyperscalers can bring them online as they’re completed.

As a business, Fervo is still in the early stages. It ended the quarter with an operating loss of $43.7 million, though it holds cash and cash equivalents of $1.9 billion and long-term debt of $301.6 million, which should give it plenty of runway.

Much will depend on the company bringing down the cost of its power plants. While hyperscalers are hungry for power today, they may not always be as price indiscriminate.

Yet if Fervo can deliver on its promises, it could end up winning on price. The first phase of its Cape Station project cost $7,000 per kilowatt of capacity, the company said. The cost for its Google project is $5,500 per kilowatt, a significant drop that suggests Fervo is on the right path. With subsidies, that’s about equivalent to a new natural gas power plant running on fuel that costs $6 per million BTUs. Still about twice what natural gas costs today, but trending closer to where it needs to be.

But if Fervo can hit its long-term target of $3,000 per kilowatt, it could significantly undercut natural gas power, even at today’s prices. That’s a pretty ambitious target, but the company has made significant strides in just a few years. No wonder Google is getting in early.

When you purchase through links in our articles, we may earn a small commission. This doesn’t affect our editorial independence.

Tim De Chant is a senior climate reporter at TechCrunch. He has written for a wide range of publications, including Wired magazine, the Chicago Tribune, Ars Technica, The Wire China, and NOVA Next, where he was founding editor.

De Chant is also a lecturer in MIT’s Graduate Program in Science Writing, and he was awarded a Knight Science Journalism Fellowship at MIT in 2018, during which time he studied climate technologies and explored new business models for journalism. He received his PhD in environmental science, policy, and management from the University of California, Berkeley, and his BA degree in environmental studies, English, and biology from St. Olaf College.

Chuck Schumer and Hakeem Jeffries Face Democratic Leadership Revolt After Midterms

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The latest conflict brewing in the Democratic Party’s cold war between the blue dogs and the insurgent progressive wing is the fight over its next congressional leaders. But with no alternate candidates stepping up to run, it’s hard to determine how real the threats to replace Senator Chuck Schumer and Representative Hakeem Jeffries actually are.

At least six incoming Democratic senators—including Michigan’s Abdul El-Sayed, South Carolina’s Annie Andrews, Minnesota’s Peggy Flanagan, Illinois’s Juliana Stratton, Kansas’s Adam Hamilton, and Maine’s Troy Jackson—have said they won’t support Schumer for majority leader. Michigan senator Elissa Slotkin has called for a “a new generation of leadership.” Even Cory Booker, who praised Schumer for helping engineer last week’s blue wave, stopped short of saying whether he would support him. On Monday, Richard Blumenthal did the same. And Jon Stewart said that the senator “does not understand the urgency of the moment and what the future demands.” Regardless, Schumer is confident. His aides say he has enough votes to become the next majority leader.

In the House, several incoming Democrats—including Claire Valdez, Angela Gonzalez-Torres, Darializa Avila Chevalier, Melat Kiros, Adam Hamawy, Chris Rabb, and Donavan McKinney—have been noncommittal with their support for Jeffries, while Ohio democrat Don Leonard has threatened to seek alternative leadership unless Jeffries changes his position on Israel.

There’s an online effort to prevent Jeffries from becoming Speaker of the House. A change.org petition demanding that House Democrats reject Jeffries as their next Speaker has attracted more than 1 million signatures as of Sunday, with actor Mark Ruffalo among those encouraging people to sign. But scroll to the bottom of the petition, and you’ll find a disclosure: Its creator, Nicole Whitted, is a paid “Change Ambassador” who receives compensation from change.org in connection with the signatures. “Thousands of organizations and brands worldwide run similar programs and in the social-cause space specifically, it’s a way to encourage people with reach and influence to use their voice for causes they care about,” Whitted told Vanity Fair in a statement.

Whitted also said she was traveling to Washington, DC next week to urge congresspeople to vote against Jeffries, and to meet with Representative Ayanna Pressley, whom Whitted told VF she’ll be supporting for Speaker. Pressley’s team did not respond to Vanity Fair’s request for comment sent on Sunday. Neither did her closest congressional allies, Rashida Tlaib, Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez, or Ilhan Omar.

Reports indicate that some congresspeople have been flooded with calls from constituents urging them to find new party leadership. But one Democratic aide told Vanity Fair that they’ve noticed what they claim is a coordinated effort to undermine Jeffries on social media, including AI-generated videos attacking Jeffries that have gotten tens of thousands of engagements.

The Brooklyn congressman does have good relationships with members of both the moderate “blue dogs” and the party’s progressive flank. Ocasio-Cortez has already backed him, and last month, Representative Ro Khanna declared that “Hakeem Jeffries is going to be Speaker.” “Don’t believe what you’re seeing on the internet,” the aide said. “It’s not reality.”

Schumer’s allies, meanwhile, have been reminding skeptics of the work he did to deliver the Democratic majority. The New York senator helped recruit candidates including Roy Cooper and Sherrod Brown, while the Democratic political operation supporting Senate candidates spent lavishly on competitive races. According to final figures provided to Vanity Fair, Senate Majority PAC and its affiliates spent nearly $407 million across races in eight states, including $150 million in Ohio, $67 million apiece in Iowa and Michigan, $65 million in Maine, and $40 million in Texas, all of which flipped. Another $14 million went toward Kansas, with smaller investments in Mississippi and South Carolina—$2.2 million and $750,000 respectively. Some of that money helped elect candidates who are now skeptical about whether Schumer should stay in charge.

In the House, each party will choose its leadership this month, but in order to become Speaker, a candidate still needs to get a majority of the entire chamber, usually 218 members, to vote for them when the new Congress convenes in January. It’s a little easier in the Senate, where the Democrat will only need to win a simple majority of their own caucus in an anonymous early December vote to become majority leader.

El-Sayed has suggested Maryland senator Chris Van Hollen, although a source close to Van Hollen told Vanity Fair that he isn’t planning to run, instead setting his sights on a potential presidential campaign in 2028. “Prior to selecting a majority leader, Senator Van Hollen believes the caucus needs to have an honest and open conversation about how we move an agenda forward that reflects the will of the American people,” says a Democratic aide familiar with the senator’s thinking. “He’s made clear that Democrats need to use their power to immediately end the illegal war with Iran, advance a robust pro-worker kitchen table agenda, and fight political corruption.”

Another name circulating among Democratic aides is Hawaii senator Brian Schatz, a relatively young progressive who has developed relationships across the party’s ideological factions and is already pursuing the number-two leadership position being vacated by retiring senator Dick Durbin.

“He’s the heir apparent,” one Democratic aide told Vanity Fair, describing Schatz as someone who could appeal to both progressives and moderates. Asked about speculation surrounding his leadership ambitions, Schatz’s spokesperson offered a noncommittal response: “I don’t have anything new to share on this but will keep you posted if that changes.”

Some Democrats are also hesitant to replace their experienced leaders after they’ve been given this narrow opportunity to get stuff done with control of both chambers of Congress. “I don’t think you’d want somebody new who’s, like, learning the ropes while we have the opportunity to really get meaningful legislation passed for the American people,” another Democratic aide said.

Still, the fighting online and on cable news exposes a tension within the Democratic Party. Moderates see the blue wave as vindication of the party’s disciplined, affordability-focused strategy. Progressives see it as a mandate for a more ambitious agenda—and a rejection of the establishment’s familiar faces.

Both factions are claiming the election as their own victory. But before they can fight over what to do with their new power, they’ll have to decide who’s going to lead them.

CNH Industrial (CNH) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kobelco JV deal brings $700 million in cash, plus tariff refund benefits.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

CNH Industrial N.V. (CNH +10.58%) reported a move toward a pure-play agriculture focus through the establishment of a construction business joint venture with Kobelco Construction Machinery. Management indicated that the company will sell a 70% stake in its construction operations for $700 million in cash, valuing the segment at an enterprise value of $1 billion. This development follows the 2021 acquisition of Raven Industries and the 2022 spin-off of Iveco Group. Despite challenging conditions in agricultural equipment markets, particularly in South America and EMEA, the company improved its industrial adjusted EBIT margin and raised its full-year guidance for adjusted diluted EPS and free cash flow. The company continues to implement cost-reduction initiatives and inventory discipline while targeting its 2030 operational objectives.

Operator: Good morning, and welcome to the CNH 2026 Third Quarter Results Conference Call. [Operator Instructions] I will now turn the call over to Jason Omerza, Vice President of Investor Relations.

Jason Omerza: Thank you, Connor, and good morning, everyone. We would like to welcome you to CNH's third quarter earnings call for the period ending September 30, 2026. This live webcast is copyrighted by CNH and any recording, transmission or other use of any portion of it without the written consent of CNH is strictly prohibited. Hosting today's call are CNH CEO, Gerrit Marx; and CFO, Jim Nickolas. They will reference the material available for download from our website. Please note that any forward-looking statements that we make during today's call are subject to the risks and uncertainties mentioned in the safe harbor statement included in the presentation material.

Additional information pertaining to factors that could cause actual results to differ materially is contained in the company's most recent annual report on Form 10-K as well as other periodic reports and filings with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission. Our presentation includes certain non-GAAP financial measures. Additional information, including reconciliations to the most directly comparable U.S. GAAP financial measures is included in the presentation material. I will now turn the call over to Gerrit.

Gerrit Marx: Thank you, Jason, and welcome to everyone joining the call. We're here to talk about the third quarter results. But to kick things off, I'm pleased to share that earlier today, we announced an agreement to establish CNH's construction business as a joint venture with Kobelco Construction Machinery. We'll talk more about this in a few minutes, but this is an important step as we conclude our journey of portfolio simplification and sharpening our agriculture focus. From here, we build an even stronger agriculture technology player organically through partnerships and through disciplined M&A.

Third quarter results reflect continued diligent execution as we manage through an agricultural equipment cycle that remains challenging, but beginning to show more constructive signals in North America. As we discussed last quarter, we continue to monitor several indicators that help us assess the timing and strength of the next up cycle. Dealers' new and used inventories, the price spread between new and used equipment, commodity prices and farmer income. Generally speaking, CNH dealers used inventories are below -- are at or below pandemic levels. And during the third quarter, we saw new inventories intentionally decline by approximately $100 million sequentially.

Model year 2027 order intake in North America is in line with expectations but conditions in EMEA and Brazil remain challenging. Farmer profitability continues to be squeezed due to low commodity prices and high input costs. This has been compounded in EMEA due to hot dry weather and in Brazil due to low credit availability. We will see what the recent and upcoming elections will add in terms of certainty, opportunity and support for our farmers. We continue to execute our self-help actions and track towards our path to 2030 objectives. Operationally, we are delivering better quality, achieving operational efficiencies and focusing on supply chain readiness.

We are also continuing to invest organically and inorganically in the products and technologies that strengthen our competitive position through the cycle. We announced an example of a strong and mutually beneficial partnership and strategic alliance with Bourgault Industries at the end of August that expands our global seeding portfolio. This is another proof point for how we bring strong technology to customers faster by combining leading innovation with CNH brands, reach and dealer network. We invest in upgrading our strongholds and future core technologies, partner with great players who complement our lineup and seek out inorganic opportunities to accelerate our transformation as we deliver on our Investor Day commitments. A farmer's freedom of choice is nonnegotiable for us.

We link that with our technology focus on soil health, along with a view of community health that we take jointly with our dealer partners. Together, this is what we call Total Farm Health. Turning to the results. Consolidated revenues were $4.4 billion, down 1% year-over-year. Industrial net sales were $3.7 billion, approximately flat year-over-year. Our Ag segment sales were down 2%, with North America up 2%, while EMEA declined 6% and South America declined 22%. The volume declines were partially offset by favorable price realization. Industrial adjusted EBIT was $202 million, up 94% and adjusted industrial EBIT margin improved 270 basis points to 5.5%. Adjusted net income was $205 million and adjusted diluted EPS was $0.16.

The 2026 results include $71 million in net IEEPA tariff refunds after some of the proceeds received were reinvested in the business for time-constrained onetime efforts that we would not have taken without the refunds. We will make further investments of these funds during the fourth quarter. Free cash flow from industrial activities was an outflow of $10 million, an improvement of $178 million year-over-year. Earlier today, we announced a definitive agreement with Kobelco to establish CNH construction equipment business as a joint venture, which we will call CE JV for now. There will be a final company name in due course.

CE JV will be a global full-line construction equipment player, 70% owned by Kobelco and well positioned to meet the needs of today's construction equipment customers. This transaction is the next step in our years-long transformation of CNH into a pure-play agriculture equipment technology and services company. Building on our acquisition of Raven Industries in 2021 and the spin-off of our on-highway operations into Iveco Group in 2022, today's news advances our portfolio simplification and sharpens our focus on agriculture.

This transaction will position CNH as the largest pure-play agricultural machinery OEM that covers the full crop cycle with a strengthened financial profile, able to innovate better and create value for farmers and for our shareholders at every point of the cycle. It also meets the brief we set out to find the right long-term partner for the construction business. As I've outlined previously and elaborated on since I came back to CNH, we were looking for a partnership that would do two things: First, upgrade our construction segment's economies of scale, its geographic reach and its competitiveness across the full product line, most notably in heavy excavators with the leading technology player.

Second, deepen the breadth and the technology of the construction machines supplied back through CNH agriculture dealer network to ensure we continue to provide farmers with a world-class lineup of construction machines for agriculture applications such as landscaping, irrigation management or even internal farm road building. It was also critical to find a trusted owner and operator that would steward construction's strong brands, high-performing teams, ambitious colleagues and excellent service and support business. We were patient and deliberate in our selection, looking for the best fit along several dimensions. We ran a comprehensive process to evaluate how best to position Construction to succeed and drive shareholder value to CNH.

As part of that, we had conversations with multiple parties over an extended period of time. There's only one CASE construction and its heritage is unique in the industry. Our discipline led us to today's agreement with Kobelco Construction Machinery, a global excavator and crane specialist with a proven track record and the technology and geographic strength that complements CNH's construction business. Kobelco is the right owner for CNH Construction for exactly the reasons I described. CE JV will become -- will combine Kobelco's expertise, geographies, technological capabilities, innovation resources and product strength with CNH Construction's brands, products and global sales and service network.

Together, CE JV creates a better resourced and focused business with the ability to be more competitive, innovative and agile in the construction industry. Through the creation of a stronger construction equipment player and CNH partner in CE JV, both CNH and CE JV dealers will benefit from a full suite of high-quality, cost-competitive construction machines that incorporate leading technology. And above all, Kobelco Construction Machinery is not a new partner for CNH. You might remember the time when we were already operating joint ventures in Europe and North America up until 2012. This next step is a new chapter in which Kobelco assumes control over the entire CASE construction business, forging a powerhouse ranking among the global top 10.

CNH will focus on serving customers with full-line agricultural applications through our 5 core brands, CASE IH, New Holland, STEYR, CNH Tech, which includes the Raven Precision Technology platform and CNH Capital. Given the agricultural use cases of many New Holland construction products, CNH will continue to distribute that brand directly in all regions except for Latin America, where New Holland Construction will operate its own network under the CE JV. The JV will be a global manufacturer and distributor of full-line construction equipment under the CASE Construction, New Holland Construction and Eurocomach brands. Kobelco will own 70% of CE JV and CNH will retain a 30% equity ownership position in CE JV for at least 3 years.

The transaction is expected to be accretive to CNH margins and strengthen our overall financial profile, enabling enhanced value creation through focused investment in our ag products and global dealer network. In addition to the cash proceeds, CNH will participate in the upside from our retained stake in what will be a much stronger construction equipment business. Leveraging Kobelco's complementary strengths, geographies, innovation and expertise, Humayun Chishti and his team will have the focus and the resources to build more competitive machines and deliver outstanding support to customers and dealers. Being with a pure-play construction leader will sharpen and accelerate their transformation. It is all about focus and execution. We expect the transaction to close by the end of 2027.

We are excited about the opportunity this transaction creates for both CNH customers and shareholders, and we are excited about the future of the construction business as it grows with Kobelco. Jim will provide some additional details later. As we work towards the closing of the CE JV transaction, we remain fully committed to our long-term agriculture strategy and to delivering sustainable value through the cycle. That strategy continues to center around 5 key pillars: expanding product leadership, advancing our iron and tech integration, driving commercial excellence, operational excellence and quality as a mindset.

As we manage through the current market environment, we obviously continue to invest in the products, technologies and capabilities that we are convinced will differentiate CNH over the long term and support the expansion of our mid-cycle margins. Today, I would like to highlight the Bourgault alliance announced in August. The Bourgault alliance is a good example of how we can expand our offering for better customer choices and bring innovation to market faster by combining a proven product leader with the reach of our brands and dealer network.

Bourgault has built a strong position in seeding technology, bringing that capability together with CNH will strengthen our competitiveness and broaden our offering across precision seeding and air drill systems, high-capacity air carts, digital technology and control systems. It also supports our focus on soil health and allows us to concentrate internal investments where we can create the greatest value across agriculture. We have always championed farmers' choice with our open FieldOps system. This alliance is another demonstration of how FieldOps can connect all equipment makes, giving farmers access to data that they own and can leverage for better farm management decisions.

With that, I will now turn the call over to Jim to take us through the details of our financials and guidance.

James A. Nickolas: Thank you, Gerrit. Agriculture third quarter net sales were about $2.9 billion, down 2% year-over-year, driven by lower volumes in South America and EMEA, partially offset by growth in Asia Pacific and by favorable price realization. Adjusted gross margin was 22.3%, up from 20.6% a year ago. Agriculture adjusted EBIT increased to $173 million from $137 million, and adjusted EBIT margin improved to 6.0% from 4.6%. The improvement includes better quality-related costs, continued operational execution and tariff refunds, partially offset by lower volumes in South America and EMEA. The net benefit of the IEEPA tariff refunds in the quarter was $32 million.

As we mentioned in our last earnings call, we do intend to reinvest most of Ag's tariff refunds back into the business. In Q3, we reinvested $25 million in one-off efforts to clear aged and discontinued equipment inventories and $5 million to accelerate Precision Technology R&D capacity growth. Q4 will see additional reinvestments. I'll cover that in a moment. Without the net refund benefit, Agriculture's adjusted EBIT would have been $141 million with a margin of 4.8%, an improvement from last year's 4.6%. We maintained our manufacturing schedule discipline with production hours down 11% year-over-year in the quarter and down 3% year-to-date. Dealer inventory declined by approximately $100 million sequentially in Q3.

Construction net sales were $777 million, up 5% year-over-year, driven by favorable price realization and higher volumes in North America, partially offset by lower volumes in South America. Adjusted gross margin improved 470 basis points to 19.2%. Adjusted EBIT increased to $65 million from $14 million a year ago, with adjusted EBIT margin increasing to 8.4%. The year-over-year improvement primarily reflected the net benefit of tariff refunds, lower SG&A expenses and continued operational discipline, partially offset by lower volumes in South America. The net benefit of the IEEPA refunds was $39 million in Q3 after we reinvested $8 million in discretionary programs to promote connected equipment and help clear aged inventories.

Without the net refunds, construction EBIT would have been $26 million with a margin of 3.3%, an improvement from last year's 1.9%. Financial Services reported a net loss of $77 million, primarily reflecting a $156 million noncash valuation allowance against deferred tax assets in Brazil due to ongoing elevated delinquencies. Excluding that noncash charge, adjusted net income was $79 million, up from $46 million a year ago, primarily reflecting lower risk costs in South America. Retail originations were $2.5 billion, down $200 million year-over-year, and the managed portfolio ended the quarter at $27.8 billion, down $700 million year-over-year. Delinquencies were 4.1%, primarily driven by South America.

Gross margin over average assets on book was 3.5% and return on assets was 1.3%. Our capital allocation priorities remain unchanged. First, we support organic growth and margin expansion through disciplined investments in commercial actions, operational efficiency and quality. Second, we preserve balance sheet strength and an investment-grade credit rating. In keeping with this, we plan to use the net proceeds from the construction sale to reduce debt. Third, we maintain capacity for strategic, disciplined and margin-accretive M&A. And finally, we return excess cash flow to shareholders through dividends and share repurchases. During the third quarter, we repurchased approximately $24 million worth of CNH stock at an average price of $10.53 per share.

This brings our total share repurchases through the first 9 months of the year to $87 million. Before I turn to the guidance for the remainder of the year, let me give you an update on tariffs and the IEEPA refunds. First, we now estimate the full year 2026 margin impact of tariffs, excluding the IEEPA refunds, to be 185 basis points in agriculture and 470 basis points in construction. This is based on tariffs in place as of today. For modeling purposes, you can assume the same impact in 2027. Next, as mentioned, we recognized $71 million in net refund benefits during the third quarter, $32 million in agriculture and $39 million in construction.

In the fourth quarter, we will make further reinvestments of the refunds previously received. So whereas there was a net benefit in the third quarter, expect a net investment in the fourth quarter. On the slide, you can see the approximate full year net benefit expected in each segment. In Agriculture, we will reinvest all but $5 million of the refunds. The biggest portion will be to fund some one-off efforts to clear aged and discontinued equipment inventories. We're also taking advantage of the funds to accelerate an increase in our precision technology R&D capacity. Both of these efforts help agriculture to become a more competitive business.

In Construction, the full year net refund benefit will be about $30 million after funding some discretionary programs to promote connected equipment and help clear aged inventories. The net refunds are now included in the segment and corporate guidance numbers we're about to review. For agriculture, our current 2026 industry outlook remains mixed by region and product. We have slightly adjusted down the North American industry retail forecast for smaller tractors, and we further moved down the industry forecast for South America in both tractors and combines. Globally, we still forecast agriculture industry machine retail sales to be about 80% of mid-cycle. With 3 quarters now behind us, our view of full year CNH Agriculture segment sales is unchanged.

We continue to expect net sales to be approximately flat year-over-year, including about 2% of favorable currency translation. We are narrowing the EBIT margin guidance to 5.1% to 5.4% from the prior range of 5% to 5.5%. The narrower range reflects our year-to-date performance, continued execution on cost and operational initiatives and the refund reinvestments, offsetting lower expectations in EMEA and Brazil. In Construction, global industry demand continues to be strong, particularly for heavy equipment. We have raised and narrowed our full year net sales outlook to up 8% to 10%, including about 1% of favorable currency translation.

We are narrowing the EBIT margin guidance to 2% to 2.3%, which includes the net tariff benefit, partially offset by lower profitability in South America. Putting the 2 segments together, we now expect 2026 industrial net sales to increase approximately 2% year-over-year. We are also adjusting the EBIT margin range from industrial activities to 3.4% to 3.8%. We are raising our industrial free cash flow outlook to $300 million to $450 million from $200 million to $400 million, and we now expect adjusted diluted EPS of $0.44 to $0.48 compared with $0.41 to $0.46 previously. Before I turn it back to Gerrit, I will walk through some of the key details of our transaction announced today with Kobelco Construction Machinery.

The transaction values the construction equipment business at an enterprise value of $1 billion. As Gerrit noted, the transaction is expected to be accretive to CNH's margins given the structurally stronger margin profile of the Agriculture segment compared to the Construction segment. A key financial impact of this transaction is CNH's ability to focus its organic and inorganic investments in the higher-returning agriculture business. Kobelco will acquire 70% of the equity of CE JV. CNH expects to receive $700 million in cash consideration for the sale of 70% of its business, subject to adjustments for net debt, working capital and other customary items. We intend to use the net cash proceeds to reduce debt and strengthen the balance sheet.

In addition to the cash proceeds, we will retain a 30% equity ownership position in CE JV. CNH and Kobelco are subject to a 3-year lockup period. To support a smooth business transition for dealers, customers and employees, CNH will continue to collaborate closely with CE JV. CNH Capital will remain with CNH and continue to provide a full range of retail financing, leases, rental programs and insurance products to CE JV. As a reminder, all construction-related receivables are factored to CNH Financial Services as part of our normal financial services operations, and that is expected to continue post closing. We expect the transaction to close by the end of 2027, subject to regulatory approvals and customary closing conditions.

While we believe CNH's transformation to a leading pure-play agriculture equipment, technology and services company will support progress on our path to 2030 goals. We have no changes to our 2030 targets or time line to achieve them. With that, I'll turn it back to Gerrit.

Gerrit Marx: Thank you, Jim. Let me finish with how we are thinking about the balance of the year and the setup for 2027. We continue to track the macroeconomic factors affecting the ag cycle, particularly farm profitability, commodity prices, input costs, interest rates and credit availability. Dealer inventory normalization is progressing and the order intake pace for model year 2027 products in North America has been in line with expectations. This gives us the confidence to say that 2026 will be the trough of the cycle for the North America region.

We have to be careful not to assume that certain seasonal products such as large cash crop planters or sprayers in North America speak for the entire global industry demand, however. The global ag cycle is actually the sum of many cycles differentiated by regions and crop production systems. While observing some green shoots, we remain disciplined in how we interpret that data. Our priority is to enter 2027 with channel inventories at healthier levels and production aligned with underlying retail demand so that we are positioned to respond appropriately as customer confidence and farm economics evolve. We expect agriculture dealer inventories to decline another $150 million to $250 million in the fourth quarter.

We will maintain production discipline and stay focused on aligning output with underlying demand while preparing our manufacturing footprint and supply base for the next phase of the cycle. At the same time, our margin improvement work in quality, strategic sourcing and operational efficiency continues. These actions are designed to strengthen performance through the cycle rather than depend on a market recovery. We will continue to make good use of the slow-moving markets to improve our products and operations. We will also continue our multi-brand dealership consolidation efforts across geographies where they improve coverage, dealer health and the customer experience.

We'll update our full year scorecard on each of the 5 pillars of our path to 2030 at our next call in February 2027. This concludes our prepared remarks, and we can now start the Q&A session.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Your first question is from the line of Jamie Cook with Truist.

Jamie Cook: Congrats on the news with Kobelco. I guess just the $1 billion number out there, I mean, the implied, I think, multiple is probably 8x EBITDA, maybe 9x to 10x, excluding the IEEPA benefits, which was probably better than I would have thought and the market would have thought. So just any color behind that? Is there assumed market share gains or revenue synergies? And I guess just the follow-up question on the -- any implications for stranded costs or unintended costs related to this transaction that makes us view your longer-term margin targets any differently?

James A. Nickolas: Yes. Great question, Jamie. It's Jim. On the multiple, if you look at the observed sort of LTM multiple based on sort of reported numbers, it's a 9x deal. Just add up each of the quarters of the CE segment EBITDA in the last 4 quarters, it's $114 million. It's about 9x. If you exclude from that number, the IEEPA tariff refunds and uses, it's a 10x multiple. So I think it's a fair transaction for both parties. But I think for most people will think of it in terms of a 10x multiple. As it relates to a stranded cost matter, so we won't close on this transaction for roughly another year, right?

We're going to close at the end of '27. And at that point, we'll be providing transition services to the CE JV. They will need back-office support for a period of time. And so it's likely not to face stranded costs until well into 2028. There will be some, of course, at that point, but our plan is to minimize or eliminate all those by the time we get to that point. So no impact on RemainCo or ag margins at that point going forward.

Operator: The next question is from the line of David Raso of Evercore ISI.

David Raso: I was curious if you can maybe take us around the globe, what you're seeing in your order books. I saw the comment in the slide about North America order intake modestly positive for model year '27 versus '26s. Just want to color on that. In the fourth quarter, the implied Ag margin is pretty high. It's 7.8% with all the IEEPA, without the IEEPA benefits and drags, it's 8.5%. So that's on the new. The used equipment machine levels in our dealer inventories have declined and they will continue to decline. They are also approaching healthy levels. So that factor you can consider is in a good shape for next year or the following year in particular. The other third driver is the price differential between new and used. There was a widening a couple of years ago, and that has slowed down, and now we are at a more or less constant gap between new and used, which means the value consumption in the lease rates or the value consumption for the farmers has become more predictable. And also here, the stabilizing factor is in place.

Now comes to commodity price. So we need commodity prices not only for a few weeks, and the forwards on those commodities to suggest that there is a sustainable operating margin for the farmers to make, but we need to also have an outlook at a healthy supply-demand balance in light of new global trade agreements that suggests that this is there to last. So the farmer needs not only a good season, they also need an outlook at another good season ahead. And this is currently the drag. We don't see that yet consistently helping farmers around the world to lean in on equipment rotations. So commodity prices is the lagger here at this point.

Despite some better numbers that we have seen over the course of the summer or the late summer, we need to see more of that and more consistently coming also in the form of forwards. And then the support, the governmental support is not the cycle itself, but it is helpful. And here, you know the whole package of farm bill will need to come together soon in the United States. I'm looking forward to what's coming there and also what South America under the newly elected, new and old leader in Brazil is going to offer to the farmers for the seasons ahead. So all these factors need to come together.

So that is what I can tell you in terms of cycles and replacement demand. I mean in Europe, when I look across the different countries, we have partially very different discussions going on in Europe by country when it comes to farmers. I mean our farms here in Europe, they are much more granular. They are smaller and they are much more constrained in cash and ability to invest. And we have very different political directions in the countries around Europe that gives some farmers a greater confidence to reinvest like we just have seen in the U.K.

And then there are countries like France where cost of living and other issues have actually escalated to the level that we have seen now on every media channel. And we see elections coming in France next year. So France is probably rather a question mark for next year when it comes to farm equipment. I mean, which indicator suggests that a farmer should invest in this moment of time of ambiguity and uncertainty, while there are other regions where it seems to be more prudent to do so given the framework conditions in place. So this is really where we are. And yes, I think the market will show its face as we finish this and enter next year.

NRG Energy (NRG) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Asset sale proceeds and a data center project headline the quarter.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

NRG Energy, Inc. (NRG +10.39%) reported third quarter results and announced a definitive agreement to divest a portion of its natural gas generation portfolio for $4 billion. Management stated that the transaction reflects an opportunity to monetize noncore assets at a significant valuation while focusing the company's footprint on the PJM and ERCOT markets. The company indicated that proceeds from the sale would be used to accelerate shareholder returns through an expanded share repurchase program and to strengthen the balance sheet via debt reduction. Management also reaffirmed its commitment to long-term growth targets and its strategy of pairing new dispatchable generation with growing power demand from data centers and industrial load.

Operator: Good day, ladies and gentlemen, and thank you for standing by. Welcome to the NRG Energy Inc. Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. [Operator Instructions] As a reminder, this conference call is being recorded. I would now like to hand the conference over to Mr. Brendan Mulhern, Head of Investor Relations. Sir, please begin.

Brendan Mulhern: Thank you. Good morning, and welcome to NRG Energy's Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. This morning's call is being broadcast live over the phone and via webcast. The webcast, presentation and earnings release can be located in the Investors section of our website at www.nrg.com under Presentations and Webcasts. Please note that today's discussion may contain forward-looking statements, which are based upon assumptions that we believe to be reasonable as of this date. Actual results may differ materially. We urge everyone to review the safe harbor in today's presentation as well as the risk factors in our SEC filings. We undertake no obligation to update these statements as a result of future events, except as required by law.

In addition, we will refer to both GAAP and non-GAAP financial measures. For information regarding our non-GAAP financial measures and reconciliations to the most directly comparable GAAP measures, please refer to our earnings release and the non-GAAP reconciliations and supplemental data file located in the Investors section of our website. With that, I will now turn the call over to Robert Gaudette, NRG's President and Chief Executive Officer.

Robert Gaudette: Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining us. I'll begin with the key messages on Slide 4. First, we're announcing an agreement to sell select generation assets to LS Power for $4 billion. We were not pursuing a sale. LS Power approached us with an unsolicited offer at 8.0x expected 2027 adjusted EBITDA. At that valuation, we saw a compelling opportunity to further focus our portfolio and create more value per share by redeploying the proceeds than by continuing to own those assets. Second, we're increasing planned share repurchases by $1 billion, bringing our total across 2026 and 2027 to $3 billion.

We believe NRG shares represent exceptional value at current levels, and we intend to act on that conviction. We plan to begin the share repurchases in 2026, ahead of the expected closing of the asset sale. The transaction also enables us to accelerate debt reduction, strengthening our balance sheet and increasing financial flexibility. Third, we delivered a strong third quarter. Execution across our businesses has brought our 2026 adjusted EBITDA outlook near the midpoint of our original guidance range despite a less favorable market environment than we anticipated when we established that guidance a year ago. That performance is a credit to our 17,000 employees. We've worked through difficult markets, a major acquisition and tremendous change this year.

Through it all, our people have stayed focused on safety and delivered for our customers every day. Our results are a testament to the strength of NRG's diversified businesses across regions, products and commodities. A strength that I believe the market has yet to fully appreciate. We're reaffirming guidance across all 4 financial metrics and introducing our outlook for 2027. Let me update you on the 1.2 gigawatt Texas data center project we discussed in August. My conviction in this project has only strengthened. It's designed to bring new dispatchable generation alongside new demand, anchored by a long-term customer commitment and disciplined investment standards.

This is exactly the type of contracted growth we've positioned NRG to pursue and exactly the type of responsible growth that benefits communities. Since August, we successfully addressed the remaining items. The project is on track, and I fully expect approved agreements and an announcement by year-end. Our partners' cooperation and engagement throughout this process reinforce the value of our differentiated approach to hyperscalers and data center development. We're also advancing additional project opportunities with the same customer beyond initial 1.2 gigawatts. As a company serving millions of customers, we've always understood that bringing sizable new demand onto the system requires new supply and responsible development.

That's the foundation of our BYOP strategy, and it's increasingly what policymakers and communities want to see. We will only pursue projects in communities that want this investment, and we'll work closely with them to get it right. These are places where our employees live and work, where we serve customers and where we have a long-term stake in their success. Our goal is to deliver reliable power while creating jobs, investment and lasting economic opportunity. That commitment is reflected in how we operate today. This year, our WA Parish team earned a Top Plant Water Award, and Jewett received a Federal Good Neighbor Award for its work with the community.

Our strategy hasn't changed, and the policy environment is moving in our direction. We have the customer relationships, development capabilities and operating experience to deliver the kind of project this market needs. Beyond the initial project, our pipeline of opportunities exceeds the turbine allocations and EPC capacity under our original development agreement with GE Vernova and Kiewit. Now let me turn to the details of the asset sale. The agreement covers 6 generation facilities totaling just under 4.9 gigawatts across New York, Texas and New England. All were part of the LS Power acquisition we completed earlier this year. One aspect of the valuation worth highlighting is Jack County, which carries an implied value of approximately $1,400 per kilowatt.

For perspective, we're developing 1.5 gigawatts of new natural gas generation in Texas for just above $1,000 per kilowatt. That comparison demonstrates the value we're realizing from selected existing assets while developing newer, more efficient generation at a lower cost. We're also monetizing assets in New York and New England that were not central to our original investment thesis. Rather than continuing to own every asset, we're taking advantage of an opportunity to realize that value and redeploy the capital. Equally important is what we're retaining. The 2 elements that drove strategic rationale for the LS Power acquisition remain with NRG, the entire PJM generation portfolio and CPower.

This preserves the generation scale, market exposure and demand response capabilities we set out to acquire. In ERCOT, our substantial generation fleet continues to support our residential business with additional capacity under development through the Texas Energy Fund. Turning to capital allocation. With the asset sale, we're accelerating shareholder returns. As I mentioned, we intend to begin $1 billion of share repurchases in 2026. We also plan to reduce debt by $2 billion upon closing, consistent with our commitment to maintaining a strong balance sheet. The remaining $1 billion provides flexibility to reinvest in our long-term strategy through attractive contracted growth opportunities or further reduce debt.

We expect the sale to close in the second quarter of 2027, subject to regulatory approvals. This is a compelling transaction. We are realizing attractive value for assets that were not central to our long-term strategy while retaining the portfolio and the capabilities that matter most to NRG. On Slide 6, we illustrate our generation position across PJM and Texas using the market assumptions underlying our 2027 guidance adjusted for the announced asset sale. We're comparing the electricity of our residential customers are expected to need with the generation available from our own fleet. We exclude C&I demand because we secure power separately for those customers when we sign their contracts.

In PJM, we're significantly long generation relative to residential demand with a meaningful exposure to both capacity and wholesale energy markets. That position allows us to participate in the growing demand for power with scale and diversification. In Texas, we have a large generation fleet supporting our residential business, and we're adding newer capacity through the Texas Energy Fund. Our first Texas Energy Fund plant came online in May, on time and on budget and is operating today. That's the kind of execution we expect from our teams. I also want to recognize our employees who keep our facilities operating safely and reliably every day.

Looking forward, our 2 remaining Texas Energy Fund plants are expected to bring a further 4 terawatt hours of annual generation in 2028. Both remain on budget and on schedule. And as wholesale power prices rise, more of our existing generation becomes economic to run, increasing our available output and exposure to the wholesale market upside. Following the sale, we retain meaningful market exposure in PJM, a substantial Texas generation position supporting our retail business and additional efficient capacity under development. That's a portfolio we believe is well positioned as electricity demand grows. On Slide 7, I'll discuss how we manage that exposure.

Our approach to managing risk is designed to support durable earnings and cash flow as we operate and grow the fleet. We're not trying to predict where power prices are headed. We're focused on protecting the economics of our portfolio while preserving opportunities to create value. We start with the retail load we expect to serve and the economics of our generation fleet. From there, we systematically build coverage over time, considering customer obligations, forward market prices and portfolio risk. Importantly, our hedge coverage includes generation allocated to serve our retail customers as well as positions hedged with external counterparties. This reflects how we're managing our overall generation exposure, not simply how much power we've sold to third parties.

For 2027, we're 75% hedged in ERCOT and 90% in PJM. As you look further out, we deliberately retain more open generation. As delivery approaches and retail demand becomes clearer, we progressively increase coverage. This provides greater visibility into near-term earnings and margins while preserving the flexibility to support future customer growth and participate in wholesale market opportunities. We've included this additional disclosure to give investors a clearer understanding of the protection we've established and where we retain market exposure. We've also updated our power price sensitivities in the appendix to illustrate how our portfolio is positioned to benefit from higher wholesale prices. With that, I'll turn it over to Bruce to walk through our financial results and outlook.

Bruce Chung: Thank you, Rob. We delivered a strong third quarter with all 4 of our key financial metrics increasing at least 50% year-over-year. Adjusted EBITDA was $2 billion, free cash flow before growth was $1.2 billion, and adjusted EPS was $4.68. Our results reflect contributions from the LS Power portfolio and higher capacity revenues alongside continued growth across our consumer businesses. In Home Energy, we benefited from higher retail margins and favorable weather in Texas. Smart Home continued to outperform our organic growth plan with adjusted EBITDA increasing 14% year-over-year, supported by strong customer growth and higher monthly recurring service margin per customer.

Importantly, we now expect to finish 2026 slightly below the midpoint of our adjusted EBITDA guidance range despite ERCOT power prices averaging nearly 40% below our original planning assumption year-to-date. The allocation of those proceeds is reflected in our 2027 pro forma capital allocation. Turning to 2027. These figures reflect our existing portfolio and exclude the impact of today's announced generation asset sale. Let me walk through the key drivers of our adjusted EBITDA guidance.

Starting with the original 2026 guidance midpoint of $5.575 billion, we expect $460 million of earnings growth from the additional month of LS Power ownership, continued execution of our organic growth plan and higher capacity revenues. Together, these items result in a normalized adjusted EBITDA opportunity of $6.035 billion based on the power price assumption in our long-term plan. Relative to those assumptions, our 2027 outlook reflects $185 million of net wholesale power price pressure, primarily from lower ERCOT prices, partially offset by higher PJM prices. We also expect $100 million of RGGI compliance costs in 2027, an increase of $30 million from our 2026 expectation.

After accounting for these factors, we arrive at our 2027 adjusted EBITDA midpoint of $5.75 billion. One important consideration is how our exposure to PJM power prices changes beyond next year. The LS Power portfolio came with legacy hedges for 2027, limiting our ability to fully benefit from higher PJM prices during the year. However, very little of that inherited hedge coverage extends beyond 2027. As a result, we expect the benefit from higher PJM prices to more than offset the original RGGI compliance costs in 2028 and beyond. Moving to 2027 capital allocation. This slide shows our base financial plan together with the expected allocation of proceeds from today's announced generation asset sale.

The plan also funds our common dividend and $333 million of investments across our generation and consumer businesses. In addition, we have allocated $1.13 billion toward liability management and preferred dividends, including $1 billion currently designated for debt reduction. Separately, today's announced transaction is expected to generate $4 billion in gross cash proceeds. We expect to allocate $2 billion toward debt reduction to maintain our current leverage position following closing. The next $1 billion represents the incremental share repurchase I discussed earlier.

While we intend to initiate the repurchase program in 2026, it is reflected here because it is supported by the transaction proceeds. The remaining $1 billion will be retained as cash, providing flexibility for contracted growth opportunities or further debt reduction. Turning to our per share outlook. We expect free cash flow before growth per share to increase 8% in 2027 from $14.50 at the midpoint of our original 2026 guidance to $15.60. This outlook does not reflect the impact of today's announced generation asset sale. Adjusted EPS is expected to increase more modestly from $8.90 to $9.05.

The adjusted EPS difference reflects higher interest expense and increased depreciation and amortization, including amortization associated with our investments in smart home customer growth. These expenses offset much of the benefit from higher underlying earnings and share repurchases, while the noncash nature of depreciation and amortization helps explain why our cash flow per share is growing faster than adjusted EPS. Looking beyond 2027, we remain confident in our long-term financial outlook and are reaffirming our expectation for more than 14% compound annual growth in both adjusted EPS and free cash flow before growth per share through 2030. That growth rate applies with or without the impact of today's announced generation asset sale.

Our long-term outlook applies over the full planning period and does not represent a minimum growth rate in any individual year. We have identifiable drivers supporting that outlook, including continued execution of our organic growth plan, earnings from the Texas Energy Fund projects as they enter service, greater exposure to PJM power prices and continued share repurchases. For additional context, our 5-year plan assumes relatively flat around-the-clock power prices of $52 to $53 per megawatt hour across ERCOT and PJM. While ERCOT forward prices for 2027 are below those assumptions, we see improving prices in 2028 and beyond as expected load growth develops.

Importantly, our existing plan does not require any contribution from the initial 1.2 gigawatt data center project, 2 gigawatts of PJM upgrade opportunities or additional opportunities across our broader development pipeline. We continue to see meaningful potential in these investments, but our long-term financial objectives stand on their own with upside as projects are contracted and developed. Our strategy starts with the customer that shapes the generation we own, the risks we manage and the investments we make. Paul Scholes blasts Man Utd players for ’embarrassing’ moment after Aston Villa draw

📅 2026-11-09  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Paul Scholes has urged Manchester United’s struggling players to ditch ’embarrassing’ laps of honour after games.

The Red Devils are sitting 18th in the Premier League going into the November international break after winning just one of their ten games so far this season.

The last time United were in such a low position at this stage of the season was in 1986, while their record of eight league games without a win has only happened once before in the club’s history.

Michael Carrick and the Manchester United squad walked around the pitch applauding the fans following a humbling 3-0 defeat to Bournemouth at Old Trafford at the end of last month.

Carrick was serenaded by the United fans despite the club’s dismal position but the supportive chants soon turned to boos directed at the players once the ex-England midfielder made his way down the tunnel.

The squad performed a similar lap of appreciation after Sunday’s thrilling 4-4 draw with Aston Villa but Manchester United legend Scholes was less than impressed by the gesture.

‘I tell you one thing they need to do, stop doing these laps of honour after every game,’ Scholes said on The Good, The Bad & The Football podcast.

‘What the f**k. It’s so bad. Jesus, it’s embarrassing. Every game’s like the last game of the season. Just get off!’

Fellow former Manchester United midfielder Nicky Butt added: ‘That’s pissing me off as well. A lap of honour after a draw…’

Gary Neville praised the ‘persistence’ of Carrick’s side but was also critical of the post-match scenes, claiming the players are ‘almost causing themselves emotional damage’.

‘You have got to admire their persistence, they kept going,’ Neville told NBC Sports. ‘The defensive errors were shocking and we’ll discuss that shortly.

‘But this lap around the pitch… I played for Manchester United 602 times and I always appreciated the fans at the end of the game.

‘But this pattern of them walking around the pitch after defeats by Bournemouth and a 4-4 at home against Aston Villa, it’s almost painful to watch. They’re almost causing themselves emotional damage.

‘This just can’t go on, can it? It’s absolutely desperate. Michael will be proud of them and the fans are singing his name because they love him but this is a really sorry situation.’

The hierarchy at Old Trafford are currently reviewing Carrick’s position and weighing up whether to make a change during the international break.

Eddie Howe, Julian Nagelsmann and Antonio Conte have all been linked with the job, although Manchester United could yet show more faith in Carrick.

The Red Devils, sitting just one point above bottom place in the Premier League, return to action with a daunting trip to Anfield to face arch-rivals Liverpool.

Casino Mogul Steve Wynn Sells His $70 Million Central Park Condo as Manhattan Luxury Market Heats Up

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Casino billionaire Steve Wynn has finally unloaded his Central Park duplex penthouse, making it the top luxury sale of a record-breaking week in Manhattan.

The mogul's prewar condo at 50 Central Park South—first listed in 2022 for $90 million, the most expensive in the country at the time—eventually saw its price drop to $70 million.

Even at this steep discount, it still took two years for the condo to find a taker. The duplex went into contract last week with Serena Boardman of Sotheby's International Realty as the listing agent (co-exclusive with Deborah Grubman of Corcoran).

The sale means the casino magnate failed to turn a profit on the 10,875-square-foot condo. But given that, according to Forbes, Wynn is worth $4.3 billion, he likely isn't sweating it too much.

The Billionaires Row duplex takes up the 30th and 31st floors within the 36-story Ritz-Carlton Residences, formerly the Hotel St. Moritz, which opened in 1930. The top 12 floors are the private homes.

The penthouse, formerly owned by real estate developer Christopher M. Jeffries, comes with an esteemed pedigree.

It was Jeffries' Millennium Partners that purchased the Emory Roth–designed building in 1999 and reimagined it along with the Ritz-Carlton Hotel Co. Jeffries was once married to Princess Yasmin Aga Khan, the only child of Prince Aly Khan and movie legend Rita Hayworth.

Yet the real selling point of the duplex is its sweeping Central Park views. With floors that aren't too high, from the vantage of the 45-foot long terrace, it feels as if one is sitting in the famed park's trees—a "bird's-eye perspective," as the listing puts it. Another terrace along the western wall stretches 28 feet.

With three bedrooms, four full baths, and two half-baths, the prewar unit features 10 grandly arched windows, a marble staircase, 15-foot coffered ceilings, and a living room that used to be the St. Moritz ballroom.

Modern touches include a chef's kitchen; a breakfast room with its own terrace; a primary bedroom with Central Park views, en suite bathroom, gym, and dressing room; a staff room with laundry; and a library.

As the condo sits atop the flagship Ritz-Carlton hotel, it comes with access to the amenities of the global luxury name, including the La Prairie spa and health club, a business center, the Contour Bar and Lounge, in-room dining services, and housekeeping services.

The unit has $31,362 in monthly dues on top of $15,500 in taxes.

As for why a property offering such grandeur may have taken so long to move, luxury broker Michael Reisor with Compass-affiliated Reisor.Team, who is familiar with the building, says, "Properties like this are wonderful but specific. There's a more limited buyer pool."

Despite the park views and architectural significance, it's still a century-old building, and buyers at this price range have their pick.

However, "when buyers at this level want prewar charm and detail, there is only one way to get it authentically, and that is to buy of that era," he says. "Are there people in droves wanting to pay at this level for prewar? No, but there are people who do. It's usually a more sophisticated buyer.

"They want that, 'This is classic New York.'"

The final price isn't yet known, but Reisor says, "We can assume it was right around [the ask price], if not right on."

The buyer hasn't been revealed, and listing agent Boardman did not return a call for comment.

One unique aspect of the 50 Central Park prewar is that it is a condo, not a co-op. Prewar buildings—built between the late 1880s and 1939—are typically co-ops.

In general, condos outsell co-ops each week—and after two weeks of the gap between them being slim, for the week ending Nov. 8, condos were far ahead, 34 contracts versus 7. Co-ops tend to be cheaper, with an average ask of $8.6 million last week versus $10.5 million for condos, according to Olshan Realty.

Another unusual feature of the sprawling duplex is that it has only three bedrooms. Often condos at this scale have expanded to four or more. Last week's top sale, a $31 million penthouse condo at 111 Murray St. in Tribeca, had five bedrooms.

"It's more of a psychological sticking point than an actual need," Reisor says of a high-end buyer's desire for plentiful bedrooms. "They think, 'At a certain price, I should be overflowing with bedrooms.' Even if they never need them."

Whoever snapped up the trophy unit likely wanted to be in that particular building, which is low-density, has only 11 private residences inside of a global luxury brand, and comes with a glamorous history and illustrious architect.

"It could be someone high-profile," Reisor speculates of the mystery buyer. "Someone high-profile would want something like this."

The Wynn sale was one of a slew of high-end contracts last week, with 45 signed, a big leap of 11 over the week before, and the biggest luxury sales week for the entire year. Of that, an astonishing 14 sales were priced over $10 million.

The New York City pied-à-terre tax doesn't seem to be squeezing the top end of the market. Reisor says last week's surge of luxury (over $4 million) buys can be attributed to the midterm elections.

"We always see a freeze-up before elections," he says. "The enemy to any financial market, including the housing market, is uncertainty. The biggest thing in play is that the election happened; it's over."

Additionally, he thinks that the "blue wave" was a positive for the high-end market, speculating that President Donald Trump's somewhat chastened stance on immigration is good news for monied buyers.

"This is especially relevant to New York City because of international buyers," he says, theorizing that affluent foreign buyers are wondering, "Will this be more friendly to me as an international purchaser?"

The second-highest contract of the week was a triplex penthouse with an asking price of $25.36 million. The condo, EA at 95 Madison Ave., is also in a 1912 prewar building. Although smaller at 8,355 square feet, the condo came with six bedrooms.

Formerly the Emmet Building, the Flatiron building is a quick stroll to Madison Square Park. Its Gothic facade is made from limestone and marble, reflecting the "architectural Golden Age," says the listing, which was held by Vicki Zhi Saali of Global Real Estate Ventures Group.

Did human sacrifice help lower violence? New research in Peru leaves intriguing question

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
