The actor is starting to book gigs again.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Johnny Depp was wrapped up in legal issues and personal matters during the early 2020s, with the biggest situation being his defamation case with former wife Amber Heard. The Oscar nominee received a mostly favorable verdict in 2022 and, since then, he’s been easing his way back into the entertainment industry. On that note, his latest feature is the 2026 movie schedule entry, Ebenezer. Now, amid this phase of his career, one of Depp’s most recent producers is getting real about the prospect of his comeback.

In 2023, Depp starred in the French historical drama film Jeanne du Barry, and the film sees him play the role of Louis XV. Nellee Holmes served as one of the executive producers on that film, and she recently spoke to CNN as part of a wide ranging story on Depp. Holmes was quite complimentary of the actor, calling him a “good human being.” In the producer’s estimation, Depp has forged connections with “people who trust him” and still believe in him. As for the quality of the leading man’s work, Holmes had this to say:

You cannot say anything that makes him bad. His work is his weapon.

In terms of Jeanne du Barry, the film generated headlines several years back due to being Depp’s first post-trial movie. The biopic was directed by Maïwenn, who also co-starred alongside Depp as the titular character. Ultimately, the movie debuted at the Cannes Film Festival in May 2023, and Depp received a standing ovation. Despite that praise, Maïwenn would later claim Depp was “difficult” to work with due to creative differences and personal conduct.

All that aside, though, Holmes is of the belief that Ebenezer will mark a turning point for Depp. The film was announced back in October 2025 and, at that time, it was also confirmed that horror maven Ti West would direct it. Months ago, West praised Depp’s portrayal of the titular Ebenezer Scrooge, noting that he possesses that “cadence and that musicality to the way that he performs.”. Another bit of praise came from Depp’s co-star, Rupert Grint, who simply referred to him as “so iconic.”

This holiday flick isn’t the only production Depp has on the horizon either. He’s also set to headline Day Drinker, which is a supernatural thriller film from director Marc Webb that’s set to be released amid the 2027 movie schedule. There have also been rumblings regarding a third Pirates of the Caribbean film being in the works, thanks to comments from producer Jerry Bruckheimer. It’s unknown if Depp would star in the film, but he joked about possibly not being able to grow facial hair again should he reprise Captain Jack Sparrow.

Ironically, amid talk about Depp making a “comeback” in Hollywood, he prefers not to use that term. Depp previously admitted to being confused by the word being applied to his circumstances, because he “didn’t go anywhere” to begin with. Still, it does indeed seem that the star’s career is on an upward trajectory and, all the while, he seems to have people like Nelle Holmes and Ti West in his corner.

Check out Johnny Depp in Ebenezer when it opens in theaters on November 13. Also, be on the lookout for Day Drinker, which is set to hit cinemas on March 26, 2027.

North Korean hackers create memecoins to launder funds from Binance hack: Elliptic

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The blockchain firm traced stolen funds through six new tokens on NEAR, where hackers used liquidity pools to obscure transfers between wallets.

North Korean hackers created memecoins to launder cryptocurrency stolen in the $6.4 billion Binance hack, routing roughly $430,000 through six newly launched tokens on the NEAR blockchain, according to a report from blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.

Between Nov. 5 and 7, the hackers created at least six tokens on NEAR and used liquidity pools on decentralized exchanges to move stolen funds between wallets, Elliptic said. The tokens attracted almost no outside trading and were abandoned shortly after launch.

The hackers used one set of wallets to swap stolen NEAR tokens for the new memecoins, then used another set to sell those memecoins back into the pools for NEAR. The swaps let them move funds without direct wallet-to-wallet transfers, though the transactions remained traceable onchain.

How stolen NEAR moved between wallets through liquidity pools. Source: Elliptic

Elliptic also found that five of the six token creators were funded with proceeds from earlier token sales just two to 13 minutes before the next launch, linking the tokens in a chain of transactions.

“North Korea’s launderers rarely rely on a single technique,” Elliptic co-founder Tom Robinson wrote in the report. “Instead, they layer many cheap techniques, each adding a little more delay.”

Elliptic said the same laundering technique was also used following the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in 2025, when North Korean-linked actors moved at least $24 million through a newly created token on the TRON blockchain.

At the time of Elliptic’s analysis, about 11.8 million NEAR stolen in the Binance hack remained unmoved. Elliptic valued those funds at $43.7 million.

Amazon’s New Kindle Paperwhite Is Here. Here’s What’s Different From the Last Version.

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

From slimmer designs to updated displays and charging features, here's what's changed with Amazon's latest e-readers.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. Why Trust Us?

Amazon has refreshed its Kindle lineup for 2026, introducing new versions of the popular Kindle Paperwhite and Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition. Both e-readers have notable design updates, but if you already own a previous model, you might wonder whether it's worth upgrading.

The short answer? It depends on which Kindle you're using. Compared to the 2024 models, the latest Paperwhites offer a sleeker design, updated display technology, and some welcome improvements to everyday usability. The Signature Edition, in particular, gets a few extras that make it feel more like a premium device. Here's what's changed and what's stayed the same.

Amazon Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition

The most noticeable upgrade is the design and appearance. Amazon has made the standard Paperwhite thinner and slightly lighter, reducing its thickness from 7.8 millimeters to 6.8 millimeters. It also weighs approximately 204 grams, compared to the previous model's 211 grams. That might not sound like much, but a slimmer e-reader is always welcome when you're holding it for hours or slipping it into a bag.

The redesigned display construction also creates a more seamless, edge-to-edge look. Amazon uses a new display stack with an oxide thin-film transistor backplane, designed to deliver excellent contrast, deeper blacks, and crisp text. The screen is still 7 inches with a 300-ppi resolution, so don't expect a dramatic jump in sharpness over the 2024 model.

Another small but genuinely useful change is the power button's placement. Instead of sitting along the bottom edge, where it was easy to press accidentally, it's now positioned on the upper-right side.

And if battery life is your biggest concern, there's good news: Amazon still promises up to 12 weeks on a single charge, just like the previous generation. The new model also maintains its IPX8 waterproof rating and adjustable warm lighting.

The Signature Edition receives the same thinner design and updated screen construction, but Amazon has made a few additional changes to distinguish it from the standard Paperwhite.

For starters, it now has an aluminum back instead of the previous model's plastic construction, giving it a more premium look and feel. That upgrade comes with a trade-off, though: At approximately 229 grams, the new Signature Edition is actually a little heavier than its 214-gram predecessor.

The new model also introduces pogo-pin connectors for charging and accessories. These let the Kindle connect to Amazon's new charging stand and a magnetic Page-Turn Cover with physical buttons, which could appeal to readers who miss the page-turn buttons on older e-readers.

One important difference is that the 2024 Signature Edition supported conventional Qi wireless charging, while the 2026 model switches to the new contact-based pogo-pin system.

Amazon also highlights a double-tap feature that lets you advance pages by tapping the side or back of the device, making one-handed reading a little easier.

Otherwise, you'll still get 32GB of storage, an auto-adjusting front light, and up to 12 weeks of battery life. Both generations are waterproof, too.

If you're shopping for a new Kindle rather than replacing an older one, the decision mostly comes down to whether the Signature Edition's premium features are worth an additional $50.

The standard Paperwhite starts at $199.99 and includes 16GB of storage, adjustable warm lighting, and a 7-inch glare-free display.

For $249.99, the Signature Edition doubles the storage to 32GB, adds an aluminum back and automatic brightness adjustment, and supports the new pogo-pin charging accessories. It's also available in Seaglass Green, whereas the regular Paperwhite comes in Graphite.

Both deliver essentially the same core reading experience, so casual readers probably won't miss much by sticking with the less expensive version.

If you already own a 2024 Kindle Paperwhite or Signature Edition and it's working perfectly fine, you probably don't need to replace it. You're still getting the same screen size and resolution, excellent battery life, waterproofing, and access to Amazon's massive Kindle library.

However, if you're upgrading from an older 2021 Paperwhite—or you're shopping for your first e-reader—the 2026 models are worth considering. The thinner design, improved screen construction, and updated controls make the latest lineup more appealing.

For most readers, the regular Paperwhite will be more than enough. But if you read every day, want the premium aluminum design, or like the idea of a case with physical page-turn buttons, the Signature Edition is a worthwhile step up.

Shop the New Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition

XRP Flashes 3-Fold Recovery Setup On Oversold Reading

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP Flashes 3-Fold Recovery Setup On Oversold Reading

Scattered bullish signals are crowned by the ultra-low RSI: a mark of a bounce back in 70% of cases studied.

Bitrue’s Research Institute just examined the XRP versus USDT trading pair from January 2023 through November 8, 2026, spotting a familiar oversold position. Trading 55.2% below its yearly peak of $2.58, XRP’s price setup now flashes ‘24’ on the Relative Strength Index (RSI).

That’s a deeply-oversold condition, meaning that XRP’s price is, in theory, under-valued against the broader market. On the 4-hour XRP price charts, XRP’s currently working band is between $1.03 to $1.18, the research notes. The altcoin’s also trading below the midline Bollinger Band (BOLL) of $1.14.

That creates unrealized potential due to the downward bias. An upswing above $1.18 “can quickly make short-inventory assumptions outdated”, states the report. This aligns with the AI-band top by Bitrue’s artificial intelligence (AI) based assistant. This empowers a look at XRP’s price structure without sentimental bias.

The report sets out three signals to watch: volume on up days overtaking volume on down days, on-balance volume turning higher & a daily close above $1.18. In August 2026, the exact shift in volume arrived on the same day as XRP’s price rebound, which is why Bitrue Research Institute’s study recommends watching these signals together.

The outlined three-month setup works like a swiss clock: with the 14-day RSI below chasing down levels below 35, that’s usually bullish for XRP. The study showed 18 events, including 17 with a complete 90-day follow-up window, when XRP finished higher after 90 days in 70.6% of those cases, exceeding the 53.5% in positive-return frequency.

Wallets holding at least 1 million XRP have also climbed to a 2026 high. However, the smaller time-frames showcased a divided sentiment between the top-tier investors. The Chaikin Money Flow (CMF) dipped below zero, flashing from -0.09 to -0.17 on one-hour & four-hour XRP price charts, respectively. That exposes the majority of whales selling XRP.

That kind of crypto whale sentiment has lowered the entrance to the XRP rich-list, as previously reported by DailyCoin. The altcoin’s now trading well below the SuperTrend price range between $1.27 to $1.30, while the research concluded that a median dip of 21% typically preceded a broader rebound rally.

With that structure not being immediate, a test of $1 isn’t far-fetched.

We're still waiting for Team Cherry to say something, anything at all

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Hollow Knight: Silksong has a new physical edition, and early arrivals reveal a surprise message from developer Team Cherry regarding additional content coming to the game in the months and, seemingly, years ahead.

Metroidvania marketing man Matthew Griffin reminds fans that "Hollow Knight: Silksong (Standard Edition) is arriving soon on November 10th." It comes with a user manual, a hand-drawn map, and reversible cover art. We're most interested in that user manual, which ends with a section on "Content Packs," per images shared online by fans who got their physical copy a smidge early.

"The world of Hollow Knight: Silksong will expand with free content packs," this page of the manual reads. "Prepare for even more adventures filled with new friends, new abilities, and new ferocious foes!"

The plurality of this promise is what's raised some eyebrows. After the many DLCs Hollow Knight received, there was little doubt that Team Cherry would add a truckload of content to Silksong. This is not a studio known for restraint. But how would all those ideas be expressed? Official word of several "free content packs" containing multiple "new abilities" for protagonist Hornet has predictably excited fans.

The manual continues: "In the first giant expansion, voyage across and beneath a salt-stricken ocean brimming with mystery and menace." Here again, fans linger on this being the first giant expansion for Silksong.

Hollow Knight also got multiple DLC packs – Hidden Dreams, The Grimm Troupe, Lifeblood, and Godmaster – adding bosses, items, areas, storylines, and exacting arena challenges. Previous comments from developers Ari Gibson and William Pellen suggested Silksong may receive a similar variety of additional content, with Sea of Sorrow expected to be a Grimm Troupe-like shotgun blast of experiences.

The manual concludes with a Hollow Knight: Silksong - Sea of Sorrow emblem that reads, "Coming soon." You'll note that this, like everything else Team Cherry has said, doesn't say when soon is.

Sign up to our Switch 2 Spotlight newsletter to make sure you get the very latest news on Nintendo's new console and its games every week

Last year, Team Cherry announced Sea of Sorrow and charted a 2026 launch. Apart from a February update on a port for the original Hollow Knight and a March post confirming expanded Silksong language support, the studio hasn't said anything else on its blog or social feeds all year. Pellen previously said Team Cherry would be more communicative, and we can see how that panned out. Three blog posts in a year, dare we dream?

In a read that's only slightly generous, it's possible that this blank phrasing, used in place of a definitive release date, is just generalist wording or an artifact of this physical edition's production. Maybe Team Cherry didn't want to announce anything major in such a limited product. But this does read like a step back from the 2026 target in the expansion's reveal trailer.

Team Cherry is quiet and spontaneous enough that a Sea of Sorrow shadow drop, or at least a release date with very little notice, wouldn't surprise me. Silksong's launch also came out of nowhere, giving countless other games just two weeks to flee the blast radius. But 2026 is rapidly coming to a close and we haven't heard a peep from Team Cherry about the expansion, so I also wouldn't be surprised if this manual does, as players fear, herald a delay into 2027.

Recent YouTube comments on Sea of Sorrow's trailer tell the whole story. "Team Cherry...? Where are you...?" wonders one viewer.

The vibes are very similar to those around The Duskbloods, a Nintendo Switch 2-exclusive FromSoftware game long said to be releasing in 2026. As the months flew by without any official word, a delay seemed more and more likely, though FromSoftware also notably had some negative test feedback to respond to. Lo and behold, The Duskbloods was delayed. But much like FromSoftware, Team Cherry can pretty much do what it wants, when it wants, and millions of fans will show up anyway.

Reframing How to Manage Gen Z—Could the Problem Be Us?

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 9, 2026 | Reviewed by Ekua Hagan

In my new book, The Future Begins With Z—I challenge business leaders who report that young employees are the toughest age group to manage. I suggest we reframe the issue: Instead of complaining about their attitudes, unprofessional conduct, and disrespect, we should look for any kernels of truth and foresight into the future. It’s easy for older generations to assume the world is getting worse.

No doubt, there’s a tension at play—but I wonder if we’re solving the wrong problem.

Is Generation Z genuinely difficult to manage, or are they simply less willing to quietly accept workplace norms that previous generations were taught never to question?

It is easy to describe a generation as demanding or entitled when it asks for more feedback, greater flexibility, meaningful work, attention to mental health, and for leaders to listen. But a more revealing question is why we interpret these expectations as unreasonable rather than as signals that the traditional relationship between employee and employer is shifting.

Caleb would agree. After he graduated college, he went to work for a Fortune 500 company in New York, serving on the business development team. Caleb was a talented 22-year-old who quickly saw ways the company could improve their use of AI, as well as its HR and operational systems. His supervisor, however, wasn’t interested in his ideas and told Caleb to “pay his dues” before he offered any advice. In less than a year, Caleb quit his job and has since launched three businesses of his own. He’s now leading one that leverages AI to get work done more efficiently. I believe his first boss missed out on an incredible talent.

Nicki would agree. She was 21 years old when her supervisor exposed how little he knew about his young team member. He accused her of having a poor work ethic, since she never worked a minute past 5:00 pm. She had to explain that she left at 5:00 to rush over to a side hustle, since her salary wasn’t enough to pay the bills. (She worked another side hustle on weekends as well.) Like Caleb, Nicki left the company. This turnover will continue to happen until older managers stop stereotyping and get acquainted with their young employees.

Ashton would agree. He started working as a sixth grader but saw jobs differently than his much older boss. This 11-year-old left behind a structured lawn company to start his own business after noticing his neighbors struggling with dirty trash bins. He wondered: Would someone pay to have them cleaned? He discovered they would. Within months, Ashton’s new company was earning him a high income to put in savings, and his work went viral on TikTok, showing how a young leader solving a local problem can inspire everyone.

Reframing this issue has helped so many organizations I have served. Rather than treating Gen Z as a workplace problem to solve, I ask leaders to consider what this generation might be revealing about the future of work.

Let me offer six questions to ask yourself if you’ve experienced clashes with younger teammates and have assumed they need to do all the changing:

Perhaps it’s time we seek growth over comfort and familiarity. If we do, however, it will require intentionality. I’m in Orlando today, spending time with my new grandson, Max. He’s 9 weeks old. When he’s awake, he constantly fidgets and groans, making sweet little noises and movements. Doctors say babies often do this in search of comfort. Each move is a search for comfort for their bodies or food if they’re hungry. This is a natural urge humans have all our lives—to find comfort and satisfaction. Call it "the comfort zone."

Leaders, however, must resist this natural quest for comfort and seek growth and progress. In our day, I believe this means learning from our young instead of just lecturing them. Let’s reframe how we perceive our youngest teammates. Maybe, just maybe, they’ll lead us into a better future.

France accuses Iran of supermarket fridge hacking

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fridges and freezers in hundreds of supermarkets across France were affected last month

France has accused Iran's Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC) of being behind cyber-attacks that raised the temperatures of supermarket fridges and freezers across the country last month.

Foreign Minister Jean-NoÃ«l Barrot said the attack was "unprecedented in both its scale and its level of aggression", adding those who were responsible would be hit with sanctions.

Iran has yet to respond officially, but some Iranian media outlets have dismissed the allegations as baseless.

The French hacking, which led to the destruction of tons of frozen anc chilled food in France was followed by a similar attack against supermarkets across several other European countries. It is not known if the instances are linked.

Barrot said the attack on France's supermarkets "marks the first large-scale cyber-attack aimed at sabotage on our national territory, and it constitutes a major attack on the nation's interests and the security of the French people".

He said the government had summoned the Iranian ambassador to Paris to "lodge a very strong protest against this extremely grave act".

France will take "any other necessary and proportionate measures" to respond to the incident and "deter anyone who might seek to repeat it", Barrot added.

The Paris prosecutor said an investigation had been opened into the head of an offensive cyber unit associated with the IRGC as well as his two deputies.

Laure Beccuau named the unit as Shahid Kaveh, which she said had an affiliation with the electronic and cyber centre of the IRGC.

Shahid Kaveh is the name given to a cluster of hackers thought to be directly employed by the Iranian regime.

In recent weeks, a number of commentators and politicians in Iran have argued that the country should extend any retaliation beyond the Middle East if the US and Israel resume the war they started earlier this year.

If the IRGC carried out the cyber-attacks, it remains unclear whether they were part of Iran's deterrence strategy.

On 11 October, hackers gained control of the software that regulates the temperatures of the refrigeration units used in many of France's supermarkets.

More than 100 of CoopÃ©rative U's supermarkets were hit, as well as 180 stores from IntermarchÃ© and its Netto brand. In some stores, temperatures of the fridges reportedly rose to 50C (120F) overnight.

Other major food retailers including Carrefour, Monoprix and E.Leclerc were also hit by the attack, which targeted software provided by Danish engineering firm Danfoss.

The software allows retailers to manage their cold units remotely.

Earlier this month a second wave of hacks took place, hitting more than 100 stores across Europe.

Supermarkets in Belgium and Luxembourg were affected, while a smaller number of shops were hit in countries including Sweden, Ireland, the Netherlands, Estonia and Finland.

Separately in August a small number of US military bases had fridges mysteriously malfunction, spoiling food. This incident is also being investigated as deliberate cyber-attacks, Reuters news agency has reported, citing sources familiar with the matter.

Since the waves of attacks on freezers, experts have been speculating which hacking groups could be responsible, with Iran being on a list of potential suspects.

In spite of a pair of Russian-aligned hacktivist groups claiming they were responsible on Wednesday, few believed them.

The French government has not said how it reached the conclusion that Iran was responsible, and attribution of cyber-attacks is extremely hard as hackers are able to hide their locations.

Additional reporting by Farshad Bayan, BBC Persian.

As of late, 90 Day Fiancé's Sarper Güven has been dipping his toes into the world of stand-up comedy. That venture is apparently proving to be fruitful as well, as he's allegedly making bank, if these latest court filings are to be believed. As Sarper and Shekinah continue to navigate the divorce process following their time on The Last Resort, it's being alleged that he has cleared over six figures from touring over the past year.

The latest news comes from TMZ, which reports that Shekinah is seeking thousands in monthly spousal support. It's a steep sum, even with both coming off the 2026 TV schedule where they netted pay for appearances within the TLC franchise. If these numbers are accurate, however, I'm shocked to say it might be justified.

Per a post on TMZ's Threads account, Shekinah wants $6,189 a month in spousal support, as well as reimbursement for $35,000 in legal fees. She went on to claim in court documents that Sarper's stand-up comedy netted $280,000 on their joint tax returns, with a $180,000 net profit.

I find that number more jaw-dropping than Sarper's body count. Sure, I'd previously written about him selling out comedy shows, but 90 Day Fiancé fans have witnessed a lot of his comedy already. I personally hadn't seen anything on TV that convinced me his stand-up was worth the price of admission, but it would seem some people do!

Shekinah also alleged in the court documents that her own business suffered a net loss of $55,000 due to her closing down the business temporarily to support Sarper on tour. The only thing I can think of is how ironic it is that Sarper might not be on the line for any of this had he signed the prenuptial agreement like she originally requested.

While none have ever presented a full pay stub to see, the general consensus amongst 90 Day stars is that they don't make a ton of money doing the show. To that point, Stephanie Matto alleged she only made a couple thousand dollars per episode. Also, while it's true that not all cast members are paid the same, many of them keep their day jobs in addition to doing the show.

90 Day Fiancé: The Other Way Just Hit On Andi And Nigel's Biggest Issue, And I Don't See Them Getting Over It

All this to say, while some reality shows allow their stars enough money that they can live off that alone, that doesn't seem to be the case for 90 Day stars. That makes it even wilder that Sarper has reportedly made so much money from his stand-up comedy work alone, especially when he's playing relatively small venues.

As these divorce proceedings continue, it'll be interesting to see what information may come out as a result. Shekinah and Sarper were on 90 Day Fiancé for many seasons, and I'd be curious to see what, if anything, comes out about the show as they make filings.

Neither of the two is currently on 90 Day Fiancé, but Sarper is rumored to be returning in a future season of The Single Life. As for Shekinah, she's allegedly done appearing on TLC, but we'll see if she follows through on that promise.

How Skims $148 advent calendar went from holiday gift to refund offer

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

By the time TikTok creator @wetsloppyswollen got to day eight of Skims' $148 advent calendar, she'd already opened a teddy bear keychain, hair ties, satin scrunchies, and a little foam toy with the Skims logo on it. Day eight was bobby pins, which would have been more of a surprise if day four hadn’t also been bobby pins. As one commenter put it, the whole thing was like a "box of pranks."

The Nov. 6 video racked up 65 million views in just three days, and prompted many more shoppers to post their own unboxings. "Terrible!" beauty creator Kayla Ryan cried out to her 1.6 million followers. Some creators are also showing off other brands' advent calendars, like P. Louise and H&M's, to compare.

By Nov. 8, Kim Kardashian's loungewear and lingerie brand was offering refunds.

"We understand that the calendar didn't meet expectations," a Skims representative told Entertainment Weekly on Nov. 9, adding that the company had contacted everyone who bought the box. Customers can return it for a full refund or keep it and take store credit. Despite the backlash, the advent calendar appears to be sold out — the demand is there. As it always is for Kim.

Though a surprise to many (well, most) purchasers, when Skims first released the box as part of its Holiday Shop on Oct. 29, under the "Fit & Fabric" dropdown page, shoppers could see exactly what was behind each door. Seven of the 12 days were devoted to hair accessories, including two packs of satin scrunchies, two packs of bobby pins, and a pair of headbands made from bra elastic. Four more doors held keychains, including one with a comb and another with a fuzzy compact mirror, and the last item in the mix was the Skims squishy. The original product URL now redirects to the brand's accessories page.

Nevertheless, disclosure isn't exactly the issue here. On social media, people posted mock unboxings and compared the contents to what they might find under their couch cushions or on the floor of their car. "Not the SHEIN advent calendar being better than skims," wrote one TikTok commenter. Some online are replacing the word "SKIMS" with "SKAMS."

Some fans and customers defended Kim: Beauty creator Glamzilla argued that the calendar was for "fans of SKIMS" who’d get a kick out of opening a little something from the brand each day. One X user wrote, "Tbh I was mad about it until I saw it’s literally labeled as an accessories advent calendar. They made it very obvious there would be no clothes (all advent calendars are scams imo though)."

But something that might go over well with a Skims superfan can play very differently on social media, where anyone can watch all 12 doors open and decide for themselves whether the box was worth $148.

As brands get better at making products people want to unbox on TikTok, they still owe the people who actually buy them a little respect. In this case, that means giving even the most devoted Skims fans something worth getting excited about.

Jensen Huang affair puts Nvidia's future at risk, with no clear successor to the renowned CEO

📅 2026-11-09  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's admission of an affair with a purported Chinese spy has raised questions about his future at the company, and what happens if the 63-year-old co-founder, who built the chipmaker into one of the most valuable businesses on the planet, is forced out.

Shares of Nvidia sank more than 4% on Monday, even as the broader market rallied sharply, after Huang acknowledged the affair in a message to staff on Sunday, and said that the board was launching an investigation into whether he was targeted by a foreign intelligence agency. The board said it has "confidence in Jensen," and that it has "no reason to believe that either national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised."

The New York Times first reported that the Chinese businesswoman was a spy for China's Ministry of State Security, according to people briefed on the intelligence circulated in a memo among the Trump administration in the spring. China has called the allegation a smear.

While Nvidia says Huang will continue to lead the company during the probe, pressure is rapidly mounting on the chipmaker, whose technology has powered the first four years of the generative artificial intelligence boom. Leaders from both parties are calling for briefings, and Democrats poised to take control of both houses of Congress are calling for investigations and new legislation "to stop advanced AI chips from fueling China's military," Sen. Elizabeth Warren of Massachusetts wrote in a post on X on Monday.

Huang has outsized influence over Nvidia, and is the sole face of the company with no clear second-in-command. He's also among the loudest champions of AI in the country, is critical to the financing of the broader infrastructure buildout and has a close relationship with President Donald Trump, giving Nvidia a front-row seat when it comes to discussions on AI policy, safety and regulation.

"The issue at Nvidia is that there is no clear successor to Jensen, and we had expected him to stay in this role for at least 10 more years," analysts at Melius Research said in a Monday research note.

Stacy Rasgon, an analyst at Bernstein, told CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" on Monday that, "Jensen is Nvidia. Nvidia is Jensen." He added that Huang "runs the thing pretty much top down," and "sort of pulls all the strings."

Nvidia didn't immediately respond to a request for comment.

Nvidia has long maintained a flat, non-hierarchical organizational structure. Huang has deep involvement in the company's day-to-day operations, with around 60 direct reports, and frequently serves as its spokesperson in front of world leaders and at industry conferences. In addition to being co-founder and CEO, Huang is also Nvidia's president.

However, relative to some of his founder peers in tech like Meta's Mark Zuckerberg and Elon Musk at SpaceX, Huang has minimal actual control. He owns just over 3% of the outstanding shares and is one of 11 members on a board that has no official chair.

And in the risk factors of Nvidia's financial filings, Huang isn't specifically identified as a key person. Rather, the company says, "we are highly dependent on the services of our longstanding executive team."

"Failure to ensure effective succession planning, transfer of knowledge, and smooth transitions involving executives and key employees could hinder our strategic planning, execution, and long-term success," Nvidia says.

Still, Huang's influence is undeniable, so much so that the question of succession is one that's hardly broached among analysts and investors.

Timm Schulze-Melander, an analyst at Rothschild & Co. Redburn, wrote in a Monday note that Huang could be forced to step back from the CEO role if the board's investigation reveals "something materially more serious" than an undisclosed relationship.

But Schulze-Melander said Huang's potential resignation isn't a foregone conclusion of the investigation, pointing to the importance of his leadership at the company.

Nvidia's board "needs to demonstrate independence and protect shareholders without destabilizing a company whose success is unusually dependent on its founder," Schulze-Melander wrote. "Its initial statement suggests it is attempting to achieve both."

Several analysts said the investigation could lead to governance changes at Nvidia, including putting a transition contingency plan in place.

The Melius analysts wrote that Nvidia has a "strong bench" of executive talent across its networking, hyperscale and high-performance computing businesses, along with "technical expertise" stemming from Chief Technology Officer Michael Kagan and Chief Scientist Bill Dally.

"While these folks can keep the engine running, Jensen is one of a kind, and his energy will be needed to maintain confidence in the AI trade," the analysts wrote.

— CNBC's Kif Leswing, Kristina Partsinevelos and Michael Bloom contributed to this report.

Century Communities names Jim Francescon as president

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Century Communities Inc. announced that Jim Francescon, currently executive vice president of corporate operations, will be promoted to president effective Jan. 1, 2027. In his new position, Jim will oversee homebuilding operations, land and related corporate functions, building on his work leading recent acquisitions in Texas and Tennessee.   AI Summary

Century Communities Inc. (NYSE: CCS) announced on Monday that Jim Francescon has been promoted to president, effective Jan. 1, 2027, as part of the company’s long-term succession planning process.

The company, ranked 13th by sales volume in the HousingWire Homebuilder Rankings, said Dale Francescon will continue to serve as executive chairman. Rob Francescon, who currently serves as chief executive officer and president, will remain CEO.

As president, Jim Francescon will oversee Century Communities’ homebuilding operations, land and related corporate functions and will help drive the company’s strategy and growth, according to the announcement.

“Today’s announcement reflects the implementation of a succession plan that our Board, Rob, and I have been planning and executing over the past two years,” Dale Francescon, executive chairman of Century Communities, said in a statement. “We have been very thoughtful in how we have approached succession, with the goal of best positioning Century for long-term alignment with shareholders, success and growth.”

“Jim has played an integral role in shaping our strategic direction and our growth into one of the largest homebuilders in the country, including leading our recent acquisitions that have deepened our footprint and strengthened our platform,” Rob Francescon, CEO of Century Communities, said. “I look forward to continuing to work closely with Jim and our entire executive team as we position Century for continued success.”

Jim Francescon said he is “honored to take on this role and grateful to our Board, Dale and Rob for their confidence.”

“We have built a strong operating platform and a talented team, and I am excited about the opportunities ahead as we continue to grow and deliver on our promise of A Home for Every Dream®,” he said.

Francescon joined Century Communities in 2019 and most recently served as executive vice president, corporate operations, overseeing much of the company’s corporate operations, including homebuilding, land, strategy, business development, and mergers and acquisitions.

In that role, he led the company’s acquisition strategy, including recent acquisitions of HistoryMaker Homes, Anglia Homes and Landmark Homes of Tennessee, which expanded Century’s presence in Dallas-Fort Worth, Houston and Nashville, respectively.

Before serving as executive vice president, Francescon was president of Century Living, the company’s multifamily platform, and earlier served as corporate vice president. Prior to joining Century, he worked in private equity at GoldPoint Partners, a subsidiary of New York Life Investments, and in investment banking at J.P. Morgan Securities LLC.

He holds a Bachelor of Science in business administration with a concentration in finance and real estate and a Master of Science in finance from the University of Denver. Francescon also serves as a trustee of the University of Denver, Children’s Hospital Colorado Foundation and ACE Scholarships Colorado.

What View Homes’ rebrand says about Gandhi’s operating overhaul

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

View Homes is consolidating its regional brands into a single identity as CEO Natasha Gandhi works to standardize operating practices and data systems. The company is expanding in Colorado and assessing how Hunt’s new Strata platform could support land strategy.  AI Summary

Seven months into her effort to transform View Homes from a loose collection of regional builders into a cohesive enterprise, CEO Natasha Gandhi has drawn a line between the company’s past and its ambitions.

A newly unified brand makes that promise visible. Now Gandhi and her team must overdeliver on it.

View Homes announced Monday a new corporate brand identity, unifying its formerly distinct Aspen View, Desert View, Horizon View and View Homes operations under one name. The change spans five divisions across Colorado, New Mexico and Texas and marks a milestone in the operational transformation Gandhi began after becoming CEO in March 2026.

The announcement also comes one week after View’s parent, Hunt Companies, joined with global investment manager Wafra to launch Strata Land Partners, a new land-financing platform led by former Landsea Homes president Michael Forsum. Together, these developments offer a clearer view of Hunt’s expanding ambitions in U.S. residential construction and development.

In an exclusive interview with HousingWire TBD, Gandhi said the branding initiative is part of a broader effort to prepare View for growth, strengthen operating standards, and compete across more markets and homebuyer segments.

“We want to be a premium builder, and by premium, it’s not that we’re unaffordable,” Gandhi said. “It just means that we want to be the best in every price point that we’re out there, we want to be the best in every market that we build in.”

Hunt acquired full ownership of View Homes in 2025, inheriting an established 35-year-old homebuilding business whose regional operations had developed distinct identities, systems and operating practices.

When Gandhi arrived in March, she found an organization with strong local relationships and experienced teams, but lacking the consistent management processes, reporting systems, and enterprise infrastructure needed to support the growth Hunt envisioned.

As The Builder’s Daily reported in October, Gandhi’s initial work focused on leadership changes, clearer accountability, more disciplined sales concessions, selective land investment, and a comprehensive review of enterprise technology.

The new branding establishes four standards intended to apply across the organization: exceptional customer experience, people-first leadership, design-forward homes, and premium quality across price points.

“I felt like previously, we had different standards in different markets, but now with this new brand, it really sets the stage,” Gandhi said. “These four pillars are something that is now going to be operationally executed in all our markets.”

The challenge is to translate those commitments into consistent performance without compromising the local knowledge and customer relationships that have made View’s regional businesses successful.

For a multiregional homebuilder, Gandhi notes, that means more than consistent signage and marketing. It requires shared expectations for product quality, customer service, sales execution, and management accountability, supported by reliable information and operating systems.

Gandhi is candid that the transformation remains unfinished. “This is just the start of our growth journey,” she said. “I will tell you, we’re not there yet, but we’re on the path of getting there.”

The branding initiative has prompted further scrutiny of the operational capabilities needed to support the company’s ambitions to up its game. Gandhi acknowledged that View’s enterprise resource planning systems and website are not yet up to par.

A builder seeking to expand across markets needs timely, consistent information on sales, margins, construction costs, inventory, and land commitments. Without it, management cannot easily compare performance across divisions or make informed decisions about capital allocation.

Gandhi previously outlined a 12-to-18-month enterprise technology implementation, along with efforts to standardize sales concessions and improve the quality of operating data. Those initiatives remain important to the company’s ability to scale.

View has also raised the bar in its home merchandising across markets and is developing products designed to reach beyond its traditional entry-level customer base. The objective is to preserve affordability where that remains the market opportunity while broadening View’s appeal to move-up buyers and customers seeking greater design and product differentiation.

The transformation is unfolding amid difficult housing economics. Slower sales, persistent affordability pressures, and costly incentives continue to strain builder margins, while land acquisition and development require substantial capital commitments long before homes generate revenue.

Hunt’s ownership gives View access to a larger real estate investment organization and a longer-term investment perspective. Its recent partnership with Wafra to establish Strata Land Partners adds another potentially valuable capability.

Strata launched with projects comprising approximately 1,300 single-family lots and a mandate to provide flexible land-financing solutions for public and private homebuilders and developers nationwide. Forsum has described the platform as a capital partner “built by builders, for builders,” emphasizing the need to understand the operational risks and financial requirements of homebuilding businesses.

Gandhi confirmed that View and Strata are already discussing opportunities to work together.

“We are actually already exploring opportunities to where Strata and [View] can partner together,” she said. “There’s definitely opportunity and conversations already in place.”

She did not identify specific transactions or financing structures under review.

Still, the potential relationship illustrates how Hunt’s residential investments could create complementary advantages. View brings homebuilding operations, local market relationships, and demand for future lots. Strata brings a platform designed to finance land acquisition and development while managing builders’ capital exposure.

Whether those capabilities translate into direct financial or operational advantages for View will depend on the transactions and terms the companies ultimately pursue. Strata’s mandate extends well beyond Hunt-owned businesses.

Gandhi said she has assembled the leadership team she believes can execute View’s next phase, including experienced executives recruited during the restructuring process.

“It’s your people that get you the results,” she said. “It’s your people that help you navigate a tough market.”

That leadership investment comes as View prepares to expand into Sterling Ranch, a master-planned community in Douglas County, Colorado, near Denver.

View currently operates in Northern Colorado, Colorado Springs, and Las Cruces, El Paso, and San Antonio. Sterling Ranch gives the company an opportunity to establish a stronger presence in the Denver metropolitan market, where Gandhi sees additional growth opportunities.

She said View is close to completing other land transactions in the Denver area and is pursuing opportunities in additional master-planned communities.

The expansion also offers an early test of the unified brand’s positioning. View intends to compete as a premium regional builder while maintaining a product range that serves buyers across price points.

Gandhi’s transformation is underway as many homebuilders protect cash, reduce land commitments, and prepare for the possibility that difficult conditions could persist through 2027 and 2028. View must balance those immediate pressures with Hunt’s longer-term objective of building a substantially larger homebuilding enterprise.

The company is not simply adding communities to its existing business. Gandhi is working to establish the leadership, systems, product standards, and capital discipline needed to support growth without replicating the fragmented operating structure she inherited.

“My goal, Hunt’s goal, is for View to grow into a premium regional builder,” Gandhi said. “I would like to see View be among the top 50, top 25 home builders in the next five years.”

Achieving that scale will require more than additional land and investment capital. View must demonstrate that its five divisions can operate consistently, protect margins amid changing market conditions, and deliver – or exceed – the customer experience its new brand promises.

Trump strikes a softer note on immigration; builders need proof

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Trump said immigration policy may be too tough and suggested a deal for long-term undocumented workers. Administration signals remain mixed as ICE raids and arrest targets pressure builders ahead of 2027.  AI Summary

On Sunday, President Donald Trump indicated that he is open to negotiating an immigration deal with the incoming Democratic Congress. However, despite softening post-election rhetoric, the Trump administration appears to be sending mixed signals on immigration, clouding the outlook for any potential bipartisan agreement.

The stakes of the nation’s highly contentious immigration debate remain high for homebuilders. After a surge in job site raids by U.S. Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) this summer and fall, many homebuilders say their workers, including those in the country legally, were swept up in enforcement actions.

Whether a bipartisan immigration deal materializes remains an open and pressing question for the numerous builders facing ongoing job site disruptions.

Speaking to CBS News’ 60 Minutes on Sunday, President Trump suggested his administration’s immigration policies “might be too tough” and may have contributed to last week’s election outcome.

“When you go into a hotel…and you have a woman that’s worked there as a maid, as a cleaner, as a this, as a that, as a waitress – a lot of good things, great jobs – and all of a sudden, they’re being taken out of the country. It’s tough. It’s a tough thing,” Trump said.

Trump added that it may be “doable” to reach an agreement allowing undocumented workers who have lived in the U.S. for many years, construction workers among them, to legally remain in the country.

“Get the killers out. Get the rapists out. Get all the rough ones out. Get the drug dealers out, but let the people that are here, and in some cases here for 10 and 15 years, work a deal for them,” Trump suggested.

It’s unclear whether Trump’s softer rhetoric will translate into a bipartisan immigration deal. However, his administration’s decision to promote Stephen Miller, a longtime immigration hardliner, to White House Chief of Staff suggests an agreement may be less likely than Trump implied in his CBS News interview.

And in a post on Sunday, the Official White House Rapid Response account, in a post on X, denied that Trump will push for an amnesty deal with Democrats.

However, Trump has at times shown a willingness to soften his stance on immigration, at least briefly. In June 2025, the Trump administration momentarily directed ICE to pause job site raids in sectors like agriculture, hospitality and restaurants after facing blowback from business leaders worried about staffing shortages.

Within days, however, the administration reversed course. It later set a target of 3,000 ICE arrests per day, leading to a record number of immigration arrests in recent months.

Trump has also helped kill a bipartisan deal before. In early 2024, ahead of the presidential election, he urged Republicans to vote against a Senate border package introduced by Sen. James Lankford (R-OK) that would have tightened asylum requirements, ended releases of migrants into U.S. communities while they await hearings, and let the president close the border when asylum claims surged. The bill ultimately failed.

Some homebuilding organizations, like the National Association of Home Builders, now have their eyes on a different bipartisan measure, the Dignity Act, which would allow certain undocumented workers to remain in the U.S. without citizenship. Trump’s words signaled openness to such a proposal, but the administration’s actions since the election suggest otherwise.

Vice President JD Vance, meanwhile, laid out in a post-midterms election post on X that the administration may be open to changing its approach to immigration, with less room for negotiation.

“Expect to hear any number of establishment politicians say the solution to our woes is amnesty. I’m open to the idea that we should change enforcement – by focusing more on businesses that hire illegal labor, for instance. But anyone pushing amnesty is lying to you,” Vance wrote.

For homebuilders, immigration policy and its impact on the construction labor pool remains among the biggest policy wild cards heading into 2027. However, history suggests that a bipartisan deal may be a heavy lift, as Washington hasn’t passed comprehensive immigration reform since 1986, under President Reagan.

Whether Washington breaks its four-decade drought on immigration reform, and whether enforcement eases on job sites, remains a key policy wild card to watch in 2027.

AWS Adds Single-Shot RAG and Retrieval-Only Mode to Bedrock

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Amazon Web Services announced on November 9, 2026 that Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base now supports single-shot retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and passage-only requests through AgenticRetrieveStream. An expanded 0–5 range for maxAgentIteration lets applications select source retrieval, a one-search answer, or the service’s existing multi-step agentic retrieval.

The update brings the basic RAG pattern, one retrieval followed by a generated answer, into the same streaming API used for complex searches. Single-shot RAG uses one foundation-model call for generation, while retrieval-only requests use none. AWS says single-shot operation can reduce latency and model usage relative to agentic retrieval, but the announcement supplies no measured latency or cost comparison.

A value of 0 returns matching passages without generating an answer; 1 retrieves once and generates an answer with generateResponse=true; and 2 through 5 preserve existing agentic behavior. Every mode supports multiple knowledge bases. Single-shot therefore refers to one retrieval, not a restriction to one knowledge base.

One iteration represents one foundation-model call. When generation is enabled, a limit of n reserves the final call for answer generation and allows as many as n minus 1 planning rounds. Each planning round pairs a query rewrite with a retrieval. The initial speculative retrieval runs before planning and never counts against the limit.

At 0, response generation must be off. Enabling it produces a 4xx error because an answer requires a model call. At 1, the stream carries one retrieval and one grounded answer with no planning trace events. Existing requests using 2 through 5 do not change.

The AgenticRetrieveStream API reference identifies messages, retrievers, and agenticRetrieveConfiguration as required request inputs, while generateResponse is optional. Generated answer chunks are emitted only when generation is enabled. The final result can contain retrieved items, a synthesized answer, and citations that connect spans of the answer to supporting results.

Retrieval-only operation is equivalent to calling Retrieve directly, but its response format differs: passages appear in the stream’s result event without score or location fields. Applications already using AgenticRetrieveStream can consequently obtain Retrieve-style behavior by changing a parameter instead of integrating a separate API.

For complex questions, the Amazon Bedrock User Guide describes a workflow that decomposes queries into sub-queries, checks whether the retrieved evidence is sufficient, conducts additional searches, and can expand to a full document. Agentic retrieval supports only fully managed Bedrock knowledge bases and permits up to five retrievers per request. Lowering the iteration ceiling can stop the agent sooner and potentially reduce accuracy on complex queries.

AWS illustrates the settings with a fictional bank’s internal policy assistant, not a customer deployment. A sole-proprietor documentation question uses maxAgentIteration=1, while a comparison of sole-proprietor and LLC requirements that also checks rules for nonresident owners uses 4, permitting up to three planning rounds. A request to inspect policy language uses 0 and returns source passages without a summary.

The application must select the mode; maxAgentIteration does not automatically route requests. The example assistant handles policy lookup and comparison, but it neither approves accounts nor replaces bank review and authorization.

For adoption, AWS recommends comparing single-shot RAG with the existing agentic configuration on the same knowledge base, using representative questions and expected answers. The evaluation should measure latency and request cost, verify correctness and support in retrieved passages, and include multi-document and unanswerable questions. Those results can guide selection of the lowest iteration limit that satisfies each workflow’s quality requirements.

The change makes retrieval depth an explicit per-request infrastructure decision. Direct lookups can avoid planning calls, while complex queries retain iterative evidence gathering. For enterprises, the central trade-off is whether reduced model usage preserves complete, supported answers on their own documents, rather than assuming a higher iteration limit is always better.

Google Research Showcases Language Explorer’s 7,000-Language Map

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google Research presented Language Explorer on November 9, 2026, an interactive platform for examining language geography, scripts and speaker populations. Its world overview spans about 7,000 languages and 159 scripts, with records intended to support linguistic research and language-technology development.

Developed under Google’s 1,000 Languages Initiative, the platform turns the open-source LinguaMeta dataset into a public visual interface. The announcement by Daan van Esch and Uche Okonkwo connects that interface to a broader effort to make language technologies useful across more of the world’s linguistic diversity.

The Language Explorer site supports searches by language name, country or region, and continent or subcontinent. Users can browse by speaker numbers, endangerment status or scripts, then select a map pin to open a detailed language page. Those pages bring together names, locations and identifiers including BCP-47 tags, Glottocodes and Wikidata IDs, providing consistent reference points across language resources.

Every value displayed on the site comes from LinguaMeta, an open-source repository that harmonizes language information from 19 cited sources. The 2024 LinguaMeta paper documented the underlying dataset at 7,511 languages with BCP-47 and ISO 639-3 codes, with an origin tag on every data point for traceability and correction. When sources disagreed, the authors either preferred the more authoritative source or conducted further research using academic publications, census material and constitutions.

The paper reported that machine translation, speech recognition and text-to-speech were widely available in only about 100 languages, with low-quality or nonexistent coverage for most others. That gap gives the metadata a practical role: speaker counts can help prioritize work, while language-script-locale combinations identify the exact combinations a localized system must handle. The repository’s structure preserves those combinations in JSON records; a simpler TSV summary leaves out source details, extended names, script-use tags, official-status research and coordinates.

Language Explorer also appears as an interactive installation in Real Talk! Language. Power. World. at the Museum of Communication Berlin. The museum records the exhibition opening on November 5, 2026.

Six themed rooms address language worlds, diversity, identities, contact, power and the future. The final room asks how AI and chatbots are changing communication and how researchers and activists are documenting endangered languages. An online companion began November 6, 2026, adding background material, videos and quizzes across the same six perspectives, including the relationship between AI and multilingualism. Both the Berlin exhibition and its companion are listed through February 27, 2028.

The paper’s coverage table shows substantial differences between fields. Speaker-population figures were available for 6,253 languages, or 83%, but endonyms, the names communities use for their own languages, were documented for only 1,136, or 15%. The map therefore offers a broad entry point, while the depth of information varies considerably by language and category.

Population values carry additional qualifications. The underlying methods omit second-language totals because reliable statistics were difficult to obtain, round larger speaker estimates by order of magnitude to avoid false precision, and warn that aggregating locale populations can magnify errors in the sources. The resource focuses on living spoken languages: signed, ancient, historical and constructed languages are excluded. Macrolanguages and languages classified as extinct are included, the latter because some have retained small speaker communities. Esperanto is a stated exception to the constructed-language exclusion because several language technologies already support it.

Each map pin marks a notional language centre derived from Glottolog coordinates and manual revisions. The site cautions that languages are spoken by communities that may move or cross political boundaries, so the pins should not be read as fixed territories.

There is also a maintenance discrepancy. Explorer’s FAQ describes LinguaMeta as an active project and invites researchers to contribute corrections and resources.

The containing url-nlp repository carries a GitHub archive notice dated October 6, 2026 and is marked read-only. The full, version-controlled dataset remains downloadable, but that status leaves the route for future contributions unclear as Google presents the interface to a wider audience.

Kyndryl Brings Bridge-Powered Autonomous Operations to ServiceNow

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kyndryl announced on November 9, 2026 an expanded ServiceNow partnership centered on Kyndryl Autonomous Operations for ServiceNow, a co-developed offering available through the ServiceNow Store. Powered by Kyndryl Bridge, it applies operational data and AI agents to incident analysis, risk assessment and remediation.

Kyndryl says the offering is intended to improve productivity, reduce risk and produce better business outcomes through predictive insights and automated remediation. Giovanni Carraro, the company’s global strategic alliances leader, said the focus is helping customers “move beyond experimentation, put AI to work and become trusted autonomous enterprises.”

Under the design, ServiceNow customers run operational data through Bridge to supply context across assets, configurations, dependencies, performance, events, changes, incidents, risks and resolutions. Specialized agents use that context to identify issues from failure patterns, generate evidence-based causal analysis, assess risk and provide step-by-step remediation guidance.

Insights from each resolved incident return to the platform, creating a feedback loop for subsequent automation and recommendations. The approach connects incident resolution to a broader operational memory, allowing handled issues to inform later analysis rather than treating each resolution as an isolated event.

The ServiceNow Store listing identifies dashboards for operational insights, IT health indicators, incident root-cause analysis, incident-resolution playbook generation and change-risk scoring. It also lists feedback on operational insights and server-based communication with Bridge through the Model Context Protocol.

Kyndryl reports that the broader Bridge platform, launched in 2022, is used by more than 1,400 enterprises, generates more than 16 million AI insights monthly and reduces IT incidents by up to 50%. Its operational experience is captured in more than 10,000 predefined best practices, runbooks, remediation practices and engineering actions.

Bridge unifies asset inventory, asset-performance information and engineering records of successful resolutions. Its agentic memory management is designed to preserve institutional knowledge and operational context, supporting continuous modernization.

Kyndryl describes Bridge as an agentic control plane, a layer that governs how AI agents operate across an enterprise’s technology estate. It applies enterprise policy as code at the point of action, maintains an auditable record of what each agent did and why, and requires human approval when risk warrants it. Its services catalog contains more than 190 services.

Michael Park, ServiceNow’s senior vice president of global partnerships and channels, said his company contributes the AI Control Tower and governance for safe operation, while Kyndryl contributes operational data and engineering depth, producing proven patterns customers can apply in their own environments.

The Store record adds an important timing distinction: version 26.10.16 is dated November 5, 2026 and labeled as the first ServiceNow Store release. The November 9 announcement therefore followed the initial Store release; it documents the expanded partnership and the offering’s availability rather than the application’s first appearance.

The listing classifies Kyndryl Autonomous Operations as a paid, certified generative-AI application sold by Kyndryl Inc., compatible with ServiceNow Australia Patch 7 and Zurich Patch 13. Prerequisites include ITSM Prime, ITOM Advanced with Discovery and Event Management, Now Assist components such as AI Skill Kit and Generative AI Controller, and Model Context Protocol client and server components.

ServiceNow’s licensing terms add that AI features in partner-built apps may consume Assists deducted from the customer’s account, and that partners must provide an estimate of how many Assists their apps may consume.

For customers, the expansion connects operational context, AI-generated analysis and governed remediation within ServiceNow’s IT workflows. Bridge contributes data, resolution practices, auditability and approval controls, while ServiceNow contributes the AI workflows and platform. That makes implementation and governance central to adoption; the operational case rests on whether the incident feedback loop can turn Bridge’s accumulated knowledge into consistently better remediation.

Elizabeth Warren Plans Prediction Market Crackdown as She Prepares to Chair Senate Banking

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sen. Elizabeth Warren plans to crack down on prediction markets once she takes the Senate Banking Committee gavel in January, with a banking aide telling Punchbowl News that Warren would seek to stop the sector’s “Wild West-style lack of oversight.”

The Massachusetts Democrat is poised to chair the panel after Democrats swept both chambers of Congress last week, putting her in charge of a portfolio that covers banking, housing, the Federal Reserve and cryptocurrency. Punchbowl published the aide’s rundown of her agenda on Monday.

Warren intends to use the committee’s investigative powers for oversight of prediction markets and election betting platforms, aimed at insider trading and platform manipulation — the same fight she opened before the midterms.

That earlier push was bipartisan. On Oct. 28, Warren and Sen. John Curtis, R-Utah, wrote to Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour and Polymarket CEO Shayne Coplan asking how the companies stop candidates, campaign staff and others with material nonpublic information from trading election contracts.

“We are particularly troubled by election-related event contracts,” the senators wrote. “Event contracts tied to elections receive prominent coverage and are treated by some observers as indicators of political momentum. There are also documented cases of candidates betting on their own races, which undermines the overall integrity of the American electoral process.”

The letter cited candidates who traded on their own contests on Kalshi, including a February 2026 California gubernatorial race, and pointed to research alleging wash trading and volume manipulation on Polymarket. It also referenced an NPR investigation of campaign insiders who said they traded ahead of polling releases.

“The identification of isolated instances of improper trading does not demonstrate that these markets are adequately protected,” Warren and Curtis wrote. “Instead, it raises serious questions about how much similar activity may be going undetected.” They asked for written answers by Election Day, Nov. 3.

Kalshi’s head of enforcement, Robert DeNault, told Business Insider that the company “proactively blocks candidates and campaign staff from trading to protect market integrity.” Polymarket did not respond to that request for comment.

Prediction markets are not a side show for bitcoin readers. Polymarket is a crypto-native venue that settled U.S. access through a CFTC-registered exchange after years offshore, while Kalshi operates as a designated contract market under the Commodity Futures Trading Commission. Both sit in the orbit of the same committee Warren is about to run.

Under Chairman Michael S. Selig, the CFTC has spent 2026 defending those platforms against state gambling laws, suing Wisconsin, New York and others to assert exclusive federal jurisdiction over event contracts on registered exchanges. A Democratic Banking chair targeting what her aide called a Wild West lands as the counterweight to that stance.

Warren has been one of crypto’s sharpest critics on the Hill. She opposed the CLARITY Act market structure bill, which failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15, and analysts have argued a Democratic Congress leaves little path for a Republican rewrite. House Democrats are already sketching their own package with a bigger SEC role.

The aide’s broader agenda for Warren also includes affordability legislation, a push to cap credit card interest rates, AI chip export controls, and oversight of Trump administration officials, Wall Street CEOs, Big Tech executives and anyone tied to the Trump family’s crypto ventures.

The test for prediction markets is no longer whether Trump-era regulators will keep clearing the way. It is whether Warren, with subpoena power and the Banking gavel, turns the Kalshi and Polymarket letter into hearings that rewrite how election and event contracts are policed.

Boston Dynamics Turns Atlas Into a Wheeled Actuator Testbed

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Boston Dynamics detailed a wheeled Atlas experiment on November 9, 2026, after replacing the humanoid’s feet with wheels to probe actuator limits and the transfer of whole-body reinforcement learning from simulation to hardware.

The company describes the configuration as a research testbed, not a product. The experiment was designed to expose how real actuators and a learned whole-body controller behave together when the motors approach their operating limits. Accelerating, turning and stopping keep the motors under load while demanding high torque and high speed across their operating range.

Boston Dynamics said years of Spot and Stretch customer experience have shown that deployed robots encounter conditions a lab cannot fully reproduce. Atlas controls engineer Xiaoyu (Andy) Wu, who wrote the account, used the testbed to examine extreme actuation behavior and the robustness of his low-level control tuning.

Training used millions of robot simulations before the policy reached hardware. The simulation-to-hardware problem, called sim-to-real, depended on making the modeled motor reproduce measured physical behavior closely enough for the learned policy to transfer. Policies trained against an idealized motor may rehearse movements the real robot cannot perform; hardware-matched models instead support behavior the robot can reproduce.

Preparation involved matching low-level control parameters across software setups, running motor-characterization experiments and tuning the simulated motor against measurements from Atlas. The team also evaluated simplifications individually, retaining harmless approximations while avoiding those that would give the policy an unrealistic advantage during training.

The project joined actuator design, low-level motor control and whole-body reinforcement learning (RL), which are usually separate specialties on Atlas. Wu’s background is in classical motor control, and this was his first substantial RL project, placing him at the boundaries among those layers.

The researchers adapted established policy frameworks, changing the reward composition, the range of training commands and posture handling. Wu said those changes produced smooth rolling while Atlas remained stable and easy to drive, and characterized the resulting transfer to hardware as essentially perfect.

Some demonstrations began with human motion-capture clips that Wu selected for substantial foot sliding. The side-dash training converted those human foot slides into steered, rolling movements as the policy learned to use the wheels.

For the 360-degree jump, the controller tracked a motion trajectory recorded from a human jumper. Wu characterized the move as relatively low-risk despite its energetic appearance and said it succeeded on the first attempt. As the robot prepared for takeoff, its wheeled lower body rolled quickly into position before the upward push, showing a different use of traction from Atlas on feet.

The hardware behind those movements required little alteration. The only added component was a custom adapter joining an off-the-shelf scooter rim to Atlas’s existing ankle actuator. In the baseline software model, each wheel became an ankle capable of continuous rotation, allowing Wu to substitute wheels for feet without constructing a separate robot model.

Boston Dynamics said lessons from the experiment are already being adapted to Atlas’s standard configuration. Closer integration between actuator dynamics and whole-body RL is intended to improve policy robustness against failure modes and expand the robot’s capabilities. The demonstrations therefore connect an unusual research configuration directly to the control system used by the conventional bipedal machine.

Wu has also started work on policies for failed actuators, including whether reinforcement learning can generate emergent responses to those failures. The follow-on extends that reliability work to hardware whose behavior changes after a failure, testing whether learned control can respond when a real actuator departs from its expected behavior.
